# Notation Glossary — `panel-npiv-discrete`

**Stage 3, dispatch 1 (memo §10 Steps 1–2); revised through dispatch 3, round 9 (memo §10 Step 4), the
dispatch-6 fix pass (§1.5d), and Part H with the dispatch-7 fix pass (§1.5e).** Produced by the
theorist, 2026-07-29.
**Status:** binding from here on. Every later `.tex` in this project uses these symbols.

This file discharges escalation item 12 of `proof_strategy_memo.md` §9 ("notation collisions — fix
before any `.tex`"). It resolves the four collisions named in the dispatch, seven further collisions
found while writing the operator equation, two found in dispatch 2, **eleven found by the
notation-warden's dispatch-3 audit** (§1.5), **seven found by its round-8 re-sweep** (§1.5b),
**seven slot decisions taken at Step 4 — five claimed, two declined** (§1.5c), **four slots claimed
in the dispatch-6 fix pass** (§1.5d), **seventeen slot decisions at Step 6 — fifteen claimed, two
declined** (§1.5e), **three slots claimed in the dispatch-8 fix pass, with one local moved to make
room** (§1.5f), **four slots in the round-12 consolidated fix round — three claimed and one binding
declaration that withdraws a §1.6 local** (§1.5g), **seven slots in dispatch 10 for Part I — four
claimed, two renames forced by a sweep, and one write-it-out** (§1.5h), **ten slots in the dispatch-22
notation fix round — three claims, four renames, three write-it-outs, and two rows that withdraw or narrow
a rule this file states as closed** (§1.5i), and **ten slots in the dispatch-23 notation fix round — six
declaring rows, with two renames and three write-it-outs distributed across them, plus the re-decision of
COL-4 — every one of which corrects a *claim* made in dispatch 22 rather than a symbol introduced by
it** (§1.5j), and **eight slots in the dispatch-24 notation fix round — COL-4 finally executed, seven
declaring rows, three write-it-outs and one rename, one collision measured and handed on, and six
false completeness claims of dispatch 23 struck** (§1.5k). Each resolution says which
symbol keeps the letter, which is renamed, and why; from §1.5b onward each also records the sweep for the
letter being **claimed**, which is the rule §1.5b makes binding.

**One numbering note, because it is a process failure and not a notation one.** Part H's draft claimed
C45–C57 while §1.5d already held C45–C48: two dispatches ran in parallel with the *files* partitioned
between them and the *slot register* not partitioned, so both started at C45. Part H is renumbered to
C49–C65. **The register is a single sequence and must be partitioned like any other shared resource.**
**Slot inventory, so that the two records in this file cannot drift apart.** The register is a single
sequence **C1–C115**: **§1.1 holds C1–C4, §1.2 holds C5–C11, §1.3 holds C12–C17, §1.4 holds C18–C19,
§1.5 holds C20–C30 (eleven), §1.5b holds C31–C37 (seven), §1.5c holds C38–C44 (seven)** — that is,
§1.1 through §1.5c hold C1–C44 — §1.5d holds C45–C48, **§1.5e holds C49–C65 —
Part H's *new* slots** — §1.5f holds **C66–C68**, added in the dispatch-8 fix pass, §1.5g holds
**C69–C72**, added in the round-12 consolidated fix round, **§1.5h holds C73–C79**, added in
dispatch 10 for Part I (`thm:rootn`), **§1.5i holds C80–C89**, added in the dispatch-22 notation fix
round, **§1.5j holds C90–C99**, added in the dispatch-23 notation fix round, **§1.5k holds
C100–C108 (nine)** — eight added in the dispatch-24 notation fix round and C108 appended later by
Phase C, which is what that subsection's own heading records — **§1.5l holds C109** (Phase 4d),
**§1.5m holds C110, with lettered sub-slots C110a and C110b** (2026-08-27, the relaxation constant),
**§1.5n holds C111–C114** (the notation-warden round), and **§1.5o holds C115** (2026-08-27, the two
readings of `def:lfstep`(b)).
**Section-by-section the two records agree and the enumeration sums to 115**: 4+7+6+2+11+7+7 = 44,
then +4 = 48, +17 = 65, +3 = 68, +4 = 72, +7 = 79, +10 = 89, +10 = 99, **+9 = 108**, +1 = 109,
+1 = 110, +4 = 114, **+1 = 115**. *The arithmetic is
printed because
the last three times this paragraph was checked only at its endpoints it was wrong in the middle.*

> "C1–C107" and summed to 107 while **seven** further slots were live in the file below it — C108 in

Any external record quoting a
different range for Part H (for instance "C36–C65" or "C40–C65") is quoting the *whole* register, or a
grep window, and not Part H's own block.

> **ROUND-12 CORRECTION TO THIS FILE'S OWN REGISTER, and it is recorded rather than silently fixed
> because a binding file that contradicts itself about its own bookkeeping is worse than one with a gap.**
> Three inconsistencies were carried simultaneously. **(1)** This paragraph read "the register is a single
> sequence C1–C67" while §1.5f's own text read "the register as a whole runs C1–C68" and its table
> printed three rows, C66, C67, **C68**. **(2)** The header sentence above read "**two** slots claimed in
> the dispatch-8 fix pass" against §1.5f's heading "**Three** slots" and its three rows. **(3)** §1.5f's
> own summary sentence claimed both "§1.5f holds C66–C67" (here) and "C66–C68" (there). The correct count
> is **three** slots in §1.5f — C66 ($\Omega$), C67 ($\omega_n$), C68 ($\mathsf c_{16},\mathsf c_{17}$) —
> and all three statements now agree. **The diagnosis is the same one §1.5e already recorded for the
> C45–C48 collision:** the register is a shared resource and every dispatch that appends to it must
> update *both* the inventory paragraph and the section heading, not one of the two.

**One rule governs which symbol moves:** the one with fewer sites, unless it is under a binding contract
with the plan or the memo, in which case the other moves. Where a rule turned out to be unenforceable —
"$\mathcal B$ never carries a subscript", "plain $D$ is retired" — the *rule* is replaced or lifted
explicitly, with reasons, rather than patched or breached.

## 0. How to read this file

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Type** | the mathematical type: scalar / vector / function / operator / set / measure / index / matrix |
| **Domain → codomain** | for functions and operators |
| **Source** | where the symbol is fixed: plan §1 (binding contract), memo, draft, or this file |

A symbol marked **RESERVED** may not be reused for anything else, at any subscript.

**Which sections state current notation, and which record history. Added 2026-08-10, because the distinction had never been written down and a sweep cannot be run without it.**

| Section | Status | How to read a discrepancy with the corpus |
|---|---|---|
| **§2–§8** | **CURRENT.** These rows state what the corpus says now. | A discrepancy is a **defect** in this file and is repaired in place. |
| **§1.6, §1.7** | **CURRENT.** Reserved locals and live near-collision rules. | Same: repaired in place, and a rule found stated more strongly than it holds is **replaced in the open**, never patched or breached. |
| **§1.1–§1.5k** | **HISTORICAL.** Each is a *fix round*, recording collisions **as they stood when they were found** and the slot claims that resolved them. | A discrepancy is usually **not** a defect: the cell is a record. It is **marked in place** — the of-its-day form flagged, with a pointer to the current row — and **never retyped**, because rewriting the record destroys the thing the record exists to keep. C108 sets this precedent and applies it to C34, the dispatch-14 census, §1.7's $\mathcal D/\mathbf D/D$ row and §8.6. |

**Which `.tex` files this file may cite, added 2026-08-10 for the same reason.** The compiled theory corpus is the eight files `scripts/build-theory/driver.tex` inputs, **verified against that file and in its `\input` order**: `assumptions_main`, `results_s2_identification`, `results_s3_estimation`, `results_s4_asymptotics`, `assumptions_supp`, `results_s3_supp`, `results_supp`, `proofs`. **`results.tex`, `assumptions.tex` and `results_main.tex` are RETIRED undivided mirrors, compiled by no document, and all three have diverged from the corpus** — `results.tex:1201` and `results_main.tex:352` still declare the withdrawn `cor:a1cprim`; all three still print the pre-generalisation scalar contrast $\widetilde W=W-\varrho(\Delta Z)$ (`results.tex:4770`, `results_main.tex:1117`, and the scalar modulus at `assumptions.tex:733`); and the first two print the moment with the contrast on the **left**, $\psi=\Delta Z\Delta U+\widetilde W L$ (`results.tex:4773`, `results_main.tex:1120`), which `eq:psidef` has not carried since the generalisation. **Rows added or retyped from 2026-08-10 cite the corpus and never a mirror.** *Two large classes of older citation are left standing and are not defects:* **(a)** every sweep count in §1.5g–§1.5k, whose reading key defines `A` = `assumptions.tex` and `R` = `results.tex` — **those counts were taken against those files on those dates and repointing them would falsify them**; **(b)** §1.6, §1.7 and §8 site citations predating this pass, which name real sites in the mirrors and usually have a corpus twin. **Repointing (b) is a live residual and it is handed on** — it needs each object re-located in the split, which is a dispatch and not a rider on a sweep. *The measured figure, d-glossary-sync: the patterns `` `results.tex ``, `` `assumptions.tex `` and `` `results_main.tex `` return **35 occurrences at or below §1.6**, from which class (a)'s embedded site lists and this pass's own deliberate mentions of the mirrors must be subtracted by reading. **The residue is not counted here, and no number is printed for it, because the separation cannot be made by pattern** — which is §1.5g(iii)(a.3) exactly, and the reason a bare total is printed instead of an adjective.*

**One consequence, stated so that it is not mistaken for an oversight.** Several §1.1–§1.5k cells print binding objects at the spellings of their day — the pre-$\mathsf L$ scalar contrast, the binary degenerate region, the pre-2026-08-09 intertwining $\Omega$, and the contrast-on-the-left order of $q$, $\psi$ and $J$. **They are left as printed.** A reader who wants the current form of any symbol should read **§2–§8 and §1.6–§1.7, and nothing in §1.1–§1.5k**, which is what this table exists to say. *Where a historical cell's closing clause is a **rule in the present tense** rather than a record — C66's "$\Omega$ never appears without its two function arguments" is the live instance — the clause is flagged in place, because a false rule reads as live wherever it sits.*

## 1. Collisions resolved

### 1.1 The four named in the dispatch

| # | Collision | Resolution | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | **$\Phi$ ×3**: (a) bounded one-to-one transform of $\Delta Z$; (b) standard normal CDF (`orthogonal_theta.R:176`); (c) $\Phi_{CI}$, the admissible set of Lemma L1 | (a) **keeps $\Phi$** (plan §1 owns it). (b) → **$F_{\mathcal N}$**. (c) → **$\mathcal V_{CI}$**. | (a) is the plan's binding contract. (b) becomes $F_{\mathcal N}$ because CDFs in this project are $F$ with a subscript naming the law — so the rename is *type-consistent*, not arbitrary. (c) becomes $\mathcal V_{CI}$ because it is a set of candidate structural functions, and $\mathcal V$ is this project's letter for such sets ($\mathcal V$, $\mathcal V_{CI}$, $\mathcal V_n$). |
| C2 | **$D$ ×3**: (a) the draft's differentiation operator ($D^{-1}$ = integration, draft `:172`); (b) plan §15's sieve difference design matrix; (c) **Part F's perturbation direction in $L$** | (a) → **$\mathcal D$** (calligraphic operator). (b) → **$\mathbf{D}$** (bold roman matrix). (c) **keeps italic $D$**. | Distinct fonts for distinct types: calligraphic operator, bold matrix, italic function. **Round-7 amendment:** dispatch 1 retired plain $D$ outright; Part F needed it and used it, so the retirement is *lifted* rather than silently breached. Full reasoning and the residual hazard are in §8.6. **DISPATCH-10 CORRECTION TO THIS ROW, and it is a factual correction rather than a re-litigation.** The row read "$\mathcal D$ appears only in the levels-vs-derivative remark (L-5 decides for levels) and in `rem:draftrec`, so it is nearly dormant." **That is false.** A grep for `\mathcal D` returns **45 occurrences across the five live theory files** — 1 in `assumptions.tex`, 10 in `results.tex`, 16 in `proofs.tex`, 13 in this file, 5 in `theory_memo.md` — and, decisively, $\mathcal D$ is **load-bearing in a maintained assumption**: **H1** (`ass:hilbscale`) reads "the source set for $\vdagger$ is a ball of the Sobolev scale $H^{s}$ of order $s$ **generated by $\mathcal D$**", and `def:srcball`/`eq:srcball` repeats that phrase to fix the ball L-19 is proved over. So $\mathcal D$ **generates the scale in which A5(vi), A5(vi′), (P-a.1), (P-c), (P-g), `eq:entropy` and `lem:unifop` Step 4 are all written**. It is the opposite of dormant: it is one of the few symbols on which a *maintained* assumption depends for its meaning. The three-font resolution is unchanged and correct; only the dormancy claim is struck. **Diagnosis, and it is C71's again:** a status claim about usage was asserted in a binding file without a sweep, and later rounds inherited it. Under §1.5g finding (i) such a claim must now name the pattern swept — here, `\mathcal D`. |
| C3 | **$\ell_n$ vs. the iteration index** | $\ell_n$ **keeps** `assapp4`'s meaning (truncation radius for $|u|$). The Landweber–Fridman iteration counter is $m$; the discrepancy-principle stopping index is $\hat m$; the iteration cap is $\bar m_n$. | Plan §1 reserves $\ell_n$. $\hat m$ (not $m_n$) for the stopping index because it is *random* — see §5 below. **Residual hazard flagged in §6.1.** |
| C4 | **$\ell_\star$ vs. $\ell_\dagger$** | **Updated in dispatch 2 on two counts.** $\ell^\Pi_\dagger$ = the **minimum-$L^2(\Pi)$-norm** solution of $\mathcal A^\ast_{\mathrm{cond}}\ell^\Pi=\boldsymbol{-\nu/\pi}$ (a fixed reference point, unique when A2 holds). $\ell^\Pi_\star$ = the **selected** solution, any fixed element of $\ell^\Pi_\dagger+\ker\mathcal A^\ast_{\mathrm{cond}}$ produced by the selection rule of `prp:orthog`. $\ell_\star:=\mathcal W\ell^\Pi_\star$ is the observable-space image. | The memo's convention, made explicit. **(1) The right-hand side is $-\nu/\pi$, not $-\nu$** (M-1/F-12) — this row said $-\nu$ through round 2 and was the last uncorrected instance. **(2) The $\Pi$ superscript is mandatory** on the range-space objects (C17), so that $\ell^\Pi_\star$ and $\ell_\star$ are never conflated. **Every statement about $\|\partial_u\ell\|_\infty$, $E[\widetilde W^2\ell^2]$, $\sigma_q$, A2′ and the variance is about $\ell_\star$**, never $\ell^\Pi_\dagger$. |

### 1.2 Seven further collisions found in this dispatch

| # | Collision | Resolution | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| C5 | **$\mu$ ×2**: plan §1's finite signed measure on $\mathcal T$ defining the correction, vs. the memo's source-condition index (Hilbert scale, $\mu=s/(2a)$) | The **source index keeps $\mu$**. The Bierens signed measure → **$\gamma$**. | $\mu$ as a source index appears in every rate display in the memo, the critic report and all downstream targets ($\delta_n=\hat\delta_n^{2\mu/(2\mu+1)}$, the propensity frontier, `prp:phirate`, `thm:rootn`). The measure appears in one definition and one integrability footnote, and by `lem:density` it drops out of the $\theta$-side entirely. **This is a departure from plan §1's table and is escalated in `theory_memo.md` §7.** |
| C6 | **$t$ ×2**: the period index $t\in\{1,2\}$ vs. the Bierens index $t\in\mathcal T$ | $t$ is **the Bierens index only**. The two periods are written out as $1$ and $2$ — no letter is used for the period index. | There are exactly two periods; a running letter buys nothing. The levels model is written $Y_{i1},Y_{i2}$. |
| C7 | **$M$ vs. $\mathcal M$**: $\mathcal M(u,t)$ the Bierens moment family (plan §2.2) vs. $M=\sigma_{\widetilde W\partial_u\ell_\star}$ (memo §3.5) | $\mathcal M$ **kept**. $M$ **retired**: the multiplier of `prp:sharpquad` is written **$\sigma_q$** throughout, where $q(x_1,x_2)=E[\widetilde W\partial_u\ell_\star\mid X_1=x_1,X_2=x_2]$. | $M=\sigma_q$ is an identity, not a definition: by the law of iterated expectations $E[\widetilde W\partial_u\ell_\star\mid X_j=x]=E[q\mid X_j=x]$, so $\sigma_{\widetilde W\partial_u\ell_\star}=\sigma_q$. Using $\sigma_q$ removes the collision *and* puts the object into the $\nu_g/\sigma_g$ notation that the memo calls the paper's spine. The shorthand $m:=M/\pi$ is also retired (it collided with the iteration index $m$); write $\sigma_q/\pi$. **Binding on the Step-4 dispatch.**  **BREACH CLOSED 2026-08-15, AND THE WAY IT SURVIVED IS THE LESSON.** `prp:orthog`(iii)'s $(\Rightarrow)$ half abbreviated $E[L_\star\mid\Delta Z]$ to a **bare italic $M$** at six occurrences. It was *flagged and deliberately not repaired* in the comment block before `cor:centarity`'s proof ("repairing it is a separate, authorised pass"), and a later collision sweep then recorded this class as **executed** while the site survived — a flag with no ticket reads as a closure to the next sweep. Repaired by the route that note itself named and that `cor:centarity`'s proof already used: **write the object out**, $\big(E[L_\star\mid\Delta Z]\big)_{jl}$ for entries. No letter is coined; every candidate for a $p\times(\mathsf L-1)$ conditional mean is taken, and a new binding symbol in a proof that needs none would be the C25 defect. **The retirement of italic $M$ is now true rather than printed-and-breached**, the letter's live census being $\mathcal M$ (Bierens family) and $\mathsf M_\epsilon$ (`prp:stopfeas`'s $O_P$ constant) — the two declared forms C99/C100 record. *This also constrained dispatch 26's own naming*: the envelope tail radius could not be called $M_\epsilon$ in either font, and is $c_\Theta(\epsilon)$.|
| C8 | **$\eta$ ×3**: individual effect $\eta_i$; tangential-cone constant $\eta<\tfrac12$; nuisance vector | $\eta_i$ **keeps** the letter (model primitive, differenced away immediately). TCC constant → **$\eta_{\mathrm{TCC}}$**. Nuisance triple → **$\zeta=(\varphi,L,\varrho)$**. | $\eta_i$ never appears without the unit subscript; $\eta_{\mathrm{TCC}}$ never appears without its subscript; $\zeta$ is free. |
| C9 | **$p$ ×3**: $\dim(Z)$ (plan §1, RESERVED); kernel order (memo §3.3); $\dim(x)$ in `assapp5` | $p=\dim(Z)=\dim(\Delta Z)$ **kept**. Kernel order → **$p_K$** (subscript never omitted). `assapp5`'s exponent is $\dim(x_1,x_2)=2$, written as $2$. | The plan reserves $p$. `assapp5`'s "$h_x^p$" was inherited from the cross-sectional companion where $x$ was $p$-dimensional; in the panel $x=(x_1,x_2)\in\IR^2$ and $X$ is scalar, so the exponent is $2$ and has nothing to do with $\dim(Z)$. **This is a correction to the draft, recorded in `theory_memo.md` §6.** |
| C10 | **$\rho$ vs. $\varrho$**: Tikhonov ridge in the balancing solve vs. the propensity score | $\varrho$ = propensity (plan §1, RESERVED). The balancing solve's regularisation is a **truncation rank $R_n$** (truncated SVD, per §19.1); if a ridge is ever reinstated it is written $\varkappa_{\mathrm{bal}}$. Plain $\rho$ is **retired**. | $\rho$ and $\varrho$ are not visually separable in running text. §19.1 replaced the ridge by an SVD truncation, so $R_n$ is the operative object; $\varkappa_{\mathrm{bal}}$ is reserved against a return of the ridge, and never appears without its word subscript, so it cannot be confused with `assapp4`'s $\kappa_n$. |
| C11 | **$\Delta$ ×2**: the period-difference operator vs. the Dirac delta at draft `:257` | $\Delta$ = period difference, **kept**. The Dirac device is **deleted**: $W$ is binary and, under the new architecture, enters only through $\widetilde W$ and through exact cell-splitting, so no "density of $W$" is ever needed. | Removes a collision and an obsolete construct in one move. Consequence: `assapp3b` survives only in its $h_w=0$ (exact indicator) form; see `assumptions.tex`. |

| C16 | **$\pi$ vs. $\Pi$** — an undocumented near-collision where the A2 bookkeeping lives | $\pi$ = reference **density** on $\mathcal X$ (domain side); $\Pi$ = reference **measure** on $\IR\times\mathcal T$ (range side), with factors $\Pi_u,\Pi_t$ and constant $c_\Pi$. | Documented rather than renamed, because the domain/range pairing is mnemonic. **But note:** this is exactly where the round-1 error lived — the adjoint in $L^2(\pi)$ carries a $\pi^{-1}$, so A2 is imposed on $\nu/\pi$ and not on $\nu$. |

A2′(0) is **deleted**. | The two really are different objects, and round 2 bridged them by asserting the referent. `lem:adjoint`(ii)–(iii) constructs the map, proves it bounded with dense range, and proves that it commutes with the adjoint equation. The price is one strengthening of B1(iv.a), a condition on a *chosen* reference measure. **N-10 CLOSED.** |

### 1.4 Dispatch-2 additions

| # | Collision | Resolution | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| **C18** | **$L$, $\ell$, $\ell_\dagger$, $\ell_\star$, $c$ typed as scalars while $\psi$ is typed $\IR^p$-valued** — a dimensional inconsistency, not a name clash. $\psi=\Delta Z\,\Delta U+\widetilde W L$ adds a $p\times1$ vector to $L$; $J=E[\Delta Z\Delta Z']+E[\widetilde W\ell_\star\Delta Z']$ adds a $p\times p$ matrix to a $1\times p$ row unless $\ell_\star$ is $p\times1$. | **$L$, $\ell$, $\ell_\dagger$, $\ell_\star$ and $c$ are $\IR^p$-VALUED**, one coordinate per coordinate of $\theta$. The `lem:adjoint` / `prp:nophifree` / `cor:udep` statements are written for a scalar coordinate and applied coordinatewise. | A2 already says the right thing — "**each coordinate** of $\nu/\pi$ lies in $\mathcal R(\mathcal A^\ast_{\rm cond})$" — so the correction is to the type declaration and not to any assumption. Found by the `jacobian-dims-numeric` dimension spec. **Recorded as finding F-15.** Consequence for Step 4: $q$ and $\sigma_q$ are $\IR^p$-valued, so `prp:sharpquad` is stated either coordinatewise or for a fixed direction ~~$\varsigma'\psi$, $\varsigma\in\IR^p$~~ **$\vartheta'\psi$, $\vartheta\in\IR^p$ with $\|\vartheta\|=1$ (round-7 correction in place, per C25 and §8.4 item 2 — same defect as the $\sigma_q$ row, same string, found by sweeping the file rather than by the finding, which named only the $\sigma_q$ row)**. |
| **C19** | **$\varpi_u$ is new** and must not be read as a variant of $\varrho$ (propensity) or $\pi$ (reference density on $\mathcal X$). | $\varpi_u$ = the Lebesgue density of $\Pi_u$, the $u$-factor of the range measure. **Always carries the subscript $u$.** | Three round Greek letters now appear ($\varrho$, $\pi$, $\varpi$) and only the subscript separates the last from the others in running text; the subscript rule is what makes it checkable by inspection. $\varpi_u$ lives on the **range** side, like $\Pi$; $\pi$ and $\varrho$ live on the **domain** and **instrument** sides. |

### 1.5 Round-7 collisions: eleven glyphs with two or more live meanings

The notation-warden's round-7 audit found eleven. Six are resolved by moving a symbol, three by
correcting a glossary row that misdescribed the actual usage, one by lifting a retirement, and one by a
verdict that the finding was mistaken. **Every resolution below moves the symbol with the fewer sites.**

| # | Collision | Resolution | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| **C20** | **$\mathcal B$ ×3 live** (plus bold $\mathbf B$): (a) `cor:rangegap`'s $\mathcal B\Xi=\nu_{\widetilde W\Xi}/\pi$ on $L^2(P_{\Delta U,\Delta Z})$; (b) `eq:Bpi`'s $(\vartheta'\sigma_q/\pi)\cdot-2\vartheta'\tilde{\mathcal K}$ on $L^2(\pi)$; (c) the Borel $\sigma$-algebra $\mathcal B(\cdot)$ | (a) → **$\Upsilon$**. (c) → **$\mathfrak B(\cdot)$**. (b) **keeps $\mathcal B$**; $\mathbf B$ unchanged. | **The line-167 rule is replaced, not patched.** "$\mathcal B$ never carries a subscript" could not exclude $\mathcal B(\mathcal X)$, which carries an *argument* and is grammatically identical to $\mathcal B\Xi$. (b) keeps the letter because $\mathcal B_{\rm sym}$ is under a binding contract with the memo and the subscript "sym" presupposes a $\mathcal B$. (a) moves to $\Upsilon$: a Greek capital, unused, taking the Greek capital $\Xi$ as argument — the type-consistency the glossary invoked for $F_{\mathcal N}$ and $\varpi_t$. Borel moves to Fraktur, which is idiomatic and cannot be confused with an operator. |
| **C21** | **$m$ ×2**: the LF iteration counter (C3) vs. Part F's moment map $m(\tau)$ | The counter **keeps $m$**. The moment map → **$\Psi(\tau):=E[\psi(\theta_\dagger,\zeta_\tau)]$**. | C7 retired the shorthand $m:=M/\pi$ *for exactly this reason* and Part F introduced a second collision on the same letter. The counter is fixed by C3 and appears as $m$, $\hat m$, $\bar m_n$, $\varphi_m$, $\hat\varphi_m$; the moment map appears in one part. $\Psi$ is the expectation of $\psi$ — a *related* pair separated by case, which is the acceptable kind, and it is recorded in §1.7. |
| **C22** | **$r$ ×2**: the Hölder smoothness of $\varrho$ (A4(ii), and $r_n=n^{-r/(2r+d_Z)}$) vs. the perturbation direction in $\varrho$ | Smoothness **keeps italic $r$**. The direction → **sans-serif $\mathsf r$**. | The two **co-occur in one display**: `lem:secondord`(iv)'s proof bounds a term involving the direction and closes "$O_P(\delta_n r_n)$" with $r_n$ built from the smoothness. Smoothness appears at ~15 sites across `assumptions.tex`, the budget table and the glossary; the direction at ~10, all in Part F. Font separation follows the $\mathsf c$, $\mathsf k$, $\mathsf L_\Gamma$ family. |
| **C23** | **$\tau$ ×2**: A7's discrepancy constant $\tau\ge1$ vs. Part F's perturbation parameter $\tau=(\tau_1,\tau_2,\tau_3)$ and its radius $\bar\tau$ | Part F **keeps $\tau$**. A7's constant → **$\tau_{\mathrm{DP}}$**. | Two sites against thirty. The word-subscript convention is the one the glossary already adopted for $\eta_{\rm TCC}$, and for the same reason: $\tau_{\rm DP}$ never appears without it. |
| **C24** | **$\mathcal C_0$ ×2**: C3's kernel support interval vs. `cor:udep`(c)'s admissible weight class | C3's interval is **written out** as $[-c_C,c_C]$. $\mathcal C_0$ belongs to `cor:udep` alone. | Precedent C12: the compact cube was written out rather than named. The interval is mentioned twice in one assumption; the weight class is a defined object with a glossary row and a proof that it is a linear space. $c_C$ joins $c_\Phi$, $c_\Pi$. **AMENDED 2026-08-15 (dispatch 26), AND THE AMENDMENT CHANGES WHAT $c_C$ *IS* WHILE LEAVING EVERY SITE THAT READS IT UNTOUCHED.** There is no kernel support interval any more: the shipped kernel is Gaussian-based, so no finite $c_C$ made C3(i)'s support clause true, and C3(ii) now posits an **envelope** $\bar\Theta$. $c_C$ is no longer *posited* — the phrase "and a finite $c_C>0$" is gone from C3's opening line — it is **derived** at `eq:envconst` as $\lVert\bar\Theta\rVert_{L^1}/(2\mathsf c_2)$. **That is what keeps ~28 downstream sites glyph-invariant:** every display of the form $2c_C\mathsf c_2$ used to read "sup × support length" and now reads "sup × ($L^1$ mass / sup)", i.e. the same number $\lVert\bar\Theta\rVert_{L^1}$. **And the magnitude survives, which is the load-bearing check and not a nicety:** measured at $p_K=8$, $c_C=1.8216$ — the magnitude a support half-width had — so `eq:normconst`, `lem:intker`(i), `prp:hatAprop`(i) and `lem:scaleinv`'s Schur test keep their numerical content and not merely their spelling. $c_C$ stays in the $c_\Phi,c_\Pi$ family |
| **C25** | **$\varsigma$**: declared a reserved *local* bound variable (§1.6) and then made **BINDING** as the $\IR^p$ direction of `prp:sharpquad` | $\varsigma$ stays **local**. The $\IR^p$ direction → **$\vartheta$**, with $\|\vartheta\|=1$. | A symbol cannot be local and binding at once, and the binding use was declared at three sites in two files. $\vartheta$ is mnemonic — it contracts the coordinates of $\theta$, and $\vartheta'\psi$ is the scalar moment for the linear combination $\vartheta'\theta_\dagger$ — and its one prior use, as a generic integrable function in `lem:nusigma`(v), moves to $\Xi$, which *unifies* it with the existing $\Xi$ row rather than adding a symbol. |
| **C26** | **$\psi$ ×2**: the moment function (RESERVED) vs. the Laplace transform $\psi(t)=E[\varepsilon e^{t'\Phi(V)}]$ in `lem:bierenscore` | Moment function **keeps $\psi$**, which is its reserved meaning. The transform → **$\mathsf L_\Gamma(t)$**. | **The warden's finding was half right and the half matters.** It listed the moment-function uses in `results.tex` and `proofs.tex` Part F as violations; those are the *reserved* use and are correct. The genuine breach is the Laplace transform, in `lem:bierenscore`'s proof and its Part C rendering. $\mathsf L_\Gamma$ names the transform of the measure $\Gamma$ it is built from, so **no new letter is introduced**. |
| **C27** | **$D$ retired by C2, and reused** as Part F's direction in $L$ | **The retirement is LIFTED.** Three fonts, three types: $\mathcal D$ the differentiation operator, $\mathbf D$ the sieve design matrix, italic $D$ the direction in $L$. | A retirement is enforceable only while nothing needs the letter. Part F needs one that pairs with $h$ and $\mathsf r$, and every free alternative is worse: $d$ collides with $d_Z$ and with differentials, $\vartheta$ is now taken, $\iota$ and $\upsilon$ are unreadable. **The three-font rule is the one §1.8 already applies to $\mathcal B_{\rm sym}$ versus $\mathbf B$**, and here it is safer, because $\mathcal D$ appears in two remarks and $\mathbf D$ nowhere yet in the `.tex`. Lifting a declaration explicitly is the honest move; breaching it silently, which is what round 6 did, is not. |
| **C28** | **$\zeta$**: the glossary row is the defect | $\zeta_\tau$, $\zeta_\star$ (**subscripted**) = the nuisance triple. **Bare $\zeta$** = a generic function — an element of $\mathcal V$, the inner function of (P7a), the argument of `eq:jackson`. | C8 declared bare $\zeta$ to be the triple on the ground that "$\zeta$ is free". It was not: *every* bare $\zeta$ in the three files is a generic function, and the triple only ever appears subscripted. The row is corrected to the actual usage rather than the usage to the row. The sieve conditioning constant $\zeta_K$ loses its symbol in `lem:balance`'s proof, where it was a third meaning inside a citation. |
| **C29** | **$E$ ×2** (NEW, found in round 7 and missed by the audit): the expectation $E[\cdot]$ vs. the best-approximation error $E(\zeta,\mathcal S_{L_x})_{L^2(dx)}$ in `eq:jackson` | Expectation **keeps $E$**. The best-approximation error → **$\mathcal E(\zeta,\mathcal S_{L_x})$**. | Two arguments and a subscripted space do not disambiguate a symbol from the most-used symbol in the project. $\mathcal E$ is DeVore–Lorentz's own letter for the object. The infimum's bound variable also moves from $g$, which is the generic moment weight, to $\varkappa$. |
| **C30** | **$\Gamma$ vs. $\gamma$**: two finite signed measures separated only by case | **Both retained**, documented in §1.7. | $\Gamma$ is the image measure $B\mapsto E[\varepsilon\un\{\Phi(V)\in B\}]$ and lives only in `lem:bierenscore`'s proof; $\gamma$ is B1(v)'s correction measure and lives in B1, `lem:density` and `rem:Tcond`. **They never co-occur in any result, proof or assumption.** Capital-versus-lowercase is discriminable in a way that $\rho$ versus $\varrho$ (retired by C10) is not, so documentation is the proportionate response. |

### 1.5b Round-8 collisions: seven glyphs, and one shared diagnosis

The notation-warden's round-8 re-sweep found seven collisions and gave the diagnosis that organises
this table:

> **Five of the seven share one shape: a round-7 resolution *claimed a slot* — italic $D$, the letter
> $M$, calligraphic $\mathcal S$, the constant $a$, the letter $B$ — and the slot was already occupied
> by an object introduced in Part A–C. Each resolution reasoned carefully about the symbols it
> *renamed* and did not sweep for the letter it *claimed*.**

**That diagnosis is accepted without qualification, and the rule it implies is now binding:**
*before claiming a slot, sweep for the letter being claimed and not only for the letter being renamed.*
Every row below records both sweeps. The rule was applied to itself in this round: the first choice of
tag for the new admissible-representer property was **R1**, and the sweep found `results.tex:270`
carrying `\begin{remark}[R1]\label{rem:R1}` — R1 already names a *result*. The tag is **A2b**.

| # | Collision | Resolution | Rationale, and the slot sweep |
|---|---|---|---|
| **C31** | **$\mathcal S$ ×2**: the selection functional $\mathcal S\ell:=\sigma_{\widetilde W\partial_u\ell}$ of `lem:solset`(iii) vs. the spline span $\mathcal S_{L_x}$ of `eq:jackson` | The **selection functional keeps $\mathcal S$**. The span is **written out** as $\operatorname{span}(b_1,\dots,b_{L_x})$ and carries no symbol. | **C29 introduced this while fixing another collision.** It moved $E\to\mathcal E$ and $g\to\varkappa$ on the stated ground that "two arguments and a subscripted space do not disambiguate a symbol from the most-used symbol in the project" — and then wrote the space as $\mathcal S_{L_x}$ without sweeping $\mathcal S$. Both branches of §1's governing rule point the same way: the span has *more* sites (7 vs 3) and, more decisively, the selection functional is under a forward contract (glossary §4 names $\mathcal S$ as what the Step-4 sweep varies) while the span is under none. Writing it out follows C12 (the compact cube) and C24 (the kernel support interval), and it is **already the majority convention**: `lem:balance`'s statement and Step 4's opening line both print the span in full. **Slot swept:** no symbol is claimed. |
| **C32** | **$M$, retired by C7, live at two further meanings**: the multiplication operator $M:=(\vartheta'\sigma_q/\pi)\cdot{}$ inside `lem:solset`(iii)'s lower-bound chain, and a scalar constant in Step 4's counterexample | The operator becomes **$\mathcal B_{\mathrm{mult}}$** (`eq:Bmult`). The counterexample's constant is **removed**: the bump height is set to $1$, both sides of `eq:crossshift` being homogeneous of degree one in $D$. | C7 retires $M$ **in the binding voice** — "Binding on the Step-4 dispatch" — *because* $M$ named the multiplier and the multiplier is now $\sigma_q$. A Step-4 author obeying C7 reads a reinstated $M$ as $\sigma_q$; **it is not** — it is multiplication *by* $\vartheta'\sigma_q/\pi$, which is the distinction §8.4 item 2 exists to protect, inside the one chain `prp:sharpquad` must reproduce. $\mathcal B_{\mathrm{mult}}$ introduces **no new letter**: it joins $\mathcal B_{\mathrm{sym}}$ in the word-subscripted $\mathcal B$-family, and it makes $\mathcal B=\mathcal B_{\mathrm{mult}}-2\vartheta'\tilde{\mathcal K}$ and $\mathcal B_{\mathrm{sym}}=\mathcal B_{\mathrm{mult}}-\vartheta'(\tilde{\mathcal K}+\tilde{\mathcal K}^\ast)$ readable off the notation. **Slot swept:** the only word-subscripted $\mathcal B$ in any file is $\mathcal B_{\mathrm{sym}}$. |
| **C33** | **$A,B,C,F$ of `eq:biaffine`** — a *labelled*, twice-cross-referenced display whose four letters were all in use, two of them **inside the same proof** | The four coefficients become **$\Psi_{00},\Psi_{10},\Psi_{01},\Psi_{11}$**, the double subscript recording the degree in $(\tau_2,\tau_3)$. | **The warden self-flagged this as its most arguable finding and wrote out the counter-argument** ("it is scratch notation"); the counter-argument is weighed and does not survive one fact. §1.6 scopes a local symbol by "the display or proof in which it appears", and here *two* declarations of $A$ (the event of Step 4's counterexample) and *two* of $C$ (the generic constant of the "no product bound" argument) sit inside a single proof, across steps that cite one another — so the local doctrine gives no cover. $B$ was in addition a **fourth font** of a letter §1.7 declares three-font. The new names introduce no letter, and the subscript *carries the content the letters did not*: $\Psi_{jk}$ is the coefficient of $\tau_2^j\tau_3^k$, so bi-affineness is readable off the notation. **Slot swept:** $\Psi$ is already the moment map and $\Psi_L$ its weight-indexed variant; $\Psi_{jk}$ separates from $\Psi_L$ by subscript *type* — integer digits vs. a weight symbol — which is the separation §1.7 already documents for $\sigma_g$ vs. $\sigma_j(\mathcal A)$ and for $b(\cdot)$ vs. $b_1,\dots,b_{L_x}$. Recorded in §1.7. |
| **C34** | **Italic $D$ ×2**: Part F's perturbation direction in $L$ vs. the degenerate region $D=\{z:\varrho(1-\varrho)=0\}$ of Part C — *the binary form the region carried at the time; it is now $\{z:\Omega(z)=0\}=\{z:\max_l\varrho_l(z)=1\}$, see C108* | The **direction keeps italic $D$**. The region becomes **$\mathcal Z_0$** (`eq:degenreg`). | **This is C27's unswept slot.** C27 lifted C2's retirement of italic $D$ on an explicit three-font rule — $\mathcal D$ operator, $\mathbf D$ matrix, italic $D$ direction — and §8.6 contemplated only a *future* clash with $\mathbf D$. The live clash was inside the italic slot, in the same files, already. The direction has ~40 sites and a glossary row; the region has 6 and none, so the region moves. $\mathcal Z_0$ is calligraphic, which is this project's font for sets (and so discharges part of the italic-set finding), and it lives on the $\Delta Z$ side, which the letter records. **Slot swept:** $\mathcal Z$ occurs in no file. |
| **C35** | **Italic $a$ ×2**: the degree of ill-posedness ($\sigma_j\asymp j^{-a}$, plan §2.6) vs. the value $\Phi(1)$ in `rem:swintercept` | Ill-posedness **keeps $a$**. The value is **written out** as $\Phi(1)$; $\cos(ta)$ becomes $\cos(t\,\Phi(1))$. | **The glossary's own rule is breached on the symbol the rule names.** §1.6 declares the sans-serif $\mathsf c$ slot expressly to keep generic constants "from $c(z)$…, from $c_\Phi$, $c_\Pi$, and from **$a$ (the degree of ill-posedness)**", and `rem:swintercept`'s $a$ is exactly a generic constant. Writing it out is better than moving it to $\mathsf c$, because $\Phi(1)$ *names the object* and makes the oddness step ($\Phi(-1)=-\Phi(1)$) transparent; it also removes the $\cos(ta)$ juxtaposition of the RESERVED Bierens index $t$ with $a$. Third application of the C12/C24 write-it-out precedent in this round. **Slot swept:** no symbol is claimed. |
| **C36** | **Italic $A$** as an event, in two counterexamples, where the convention for sets is calligraphic | The event is **written out**: "$P(\Delta h=-1)=\tfrac12$ and $\Delta h=0$ elsewhere", with $\{\Delta h=-1\}$ in place of $A$. | The event was declared in place and died within its display, so this is the weakest of the seven — but it is one of the three italic sets the typography finding names, it collides with C33's $A$ *inside one proof*, and it costs nothing: the event *is* $\{\Delta h=-1\}$, so naming it $A$ bought a letter and lost the definition. **The third italic set, the zero set $Z_t$, is deliberately not moved**: the warden placed it in "considered and declined" as declared-in-place and dying within its step, and the `rem:ffcompare` occurrences are inside an explicitly declared verbatim-foreign-notation block, which the warden calls "the right way to do it". |
| **C37** | **`\mathrm` vs. `\operatorname`** for operator names — `supp` and `sign` set both ways | **`\operatorname` for an operator name that takes an argument; `\mathrm` for a word subscript.** | The two differ in the spacing they insert before the argument, so the same operator sets differently in the compiled document. The rule as stated keeps $\sigma_{\mathrm{ess}}$, $\mathcal A_{\mathrm{cond}}$, $\mathcal B_{\mathrm{sym}}$, $\mathcal B_{\mathrm{mult}}$, $\eta_{\mathrm{TCC}}$, $\tau_{\mathrm{DP}}$ in `\mathrm` — they are subscripts, not operators — and moves `supp`, `sign` to `\operatorname`, joining `span` and `ess ran`, which were already set that way. |

**Two findings are answered rather than actioned, and both are recorded in `theory_memo.md` §D-0000000.**
The $\bar\tau$-suppression in $\|\cdot\|_{0,h}$, $\|\cdot\|_{1,h}$ is closed by a *monotonicity* clause in
`def:dirclass` plus an explicit block on the false inference in `rem:dirmember`, rather than by a third
subscript on a glyph that already carries two indices. The undefined $\hat{\mathcal A}$ family is closed
by a definition (`def:hatA`), not a rename.

### 1.5c Round-9 (Step 4): slots claimed, slots declined, and one rule amended

Step 4 introduces symbols rather than resolving collisions, so §1.5b's rule — *sweep for the letter you
are claiming, not only for the letter you are renaming* — is the operative one, and every row below
records the sweep. **The rule caught two things before they were written, and one of them was inside the
one chain `prp:sharpquad` runs on**, which is the third round in a row that the rule has paid for itself.

| # | Slot | Disposition | Rationale, and the slot sweep |
|---|---|---|---|
| **C38** | **Italic $Q$**, which the strategy memo uses for the second-order remainder $Q(h)$ | **DECLINED. The remainder carries no symbol**; it is written as the form $\langle\mathcal Bh,h\rangle_{L^2(\pi)}$ (`eq:quadrem`). | **Slot swept, and it is occupied**: (P6b)'s regular conditional distributions $Q_1,Q_2,Q_{12}$ live at six sites in `proofs.tex`, and $Q_{12}$ is what supplies the *jointly measurable version of $q$* — so the collision would sit **inside the chain `prp:sharpquad` runs on**, which is C32's and C34's exact species. Blackboard $\IQ,\IQ_0$ (the rationals) is a third meaning, font-separated. **Writing the form out is better than any rename**: it makes the sharp constant readable off the notation, exactly as $\mathcal B=\mathcal B_{\rm mult}-2\vartheta'\tilde{\mathcal K}$ is, and it is the fifth application of the C12/C24/C31/C35 write-it-out precedent. A **blocking row** is added to `check_rendering_sync.py` so the slot cannot be claimed later by accident. |
| **C39** | **$H^{\mu''}(\IR)$**, the $u$-Sobolev scale of A2c | **Claimed, with the argument mandatory.** | **Slot swept:** $H^{s}$ is already this project's Sobolev notation (H1, A5(vi)) — so no new letter — but it denotes a *different* scale: order $s$, on $\mathcal X$, in $x$, generated by $\mathcal D$. The separation is the index letter **and** the explicit argument, and **$H^{\mu''}$ never appears without $(\IR)$**. $H^s$ is left unchanged rather than made $H^s(\mathcal X)$, on the minimal-change principle. Recorded in §1.7. |
| **C40** | **$\Lambda^{(\mu'')}$**, the source budget | **Claimed; it joins the parenthesised-superscript $\Lambda$ family.** | **Slot swept:** $\Lambda^{(0)},\Lambda^{(1)}$ are the A2b budgets; $\Lambda_1$ is B1(v)'s dominating *function*; bare $\Lambda$ is a §1.6 local. The existing row already declares the separator — "superscripts in parentheses versus a plain subscript" — and the new member is the *same* index in the same family: the superscript is the **order**, so "the budget at order $\mu''$ dominates the budgets at orders $0$ and $1$" (`eq:l21budget`) is readable off the notation. |
| **C41** | **$c_{\mu''}$**, the embedding constant | **Claimed; it joins $c_\Phi$, $c_\Pi$, $c_C$, $\bar c_\pi$.** | **Slot swept:** lowercase $c$ with a subscript is this project's constant family; capital $C$ with a subscript is a *kernel* ($C_{h_u},C_{h_x}$) and $C_{\!\mathcal A}$ is the Fréchet constant. `c_{\mu` occurs in no file. **And no symbol is given to the integral inside it** — $\int(1+\xi^2)^{1-\mu''}d\xi$ is written out, because the obvious name $\mathsf J$ sits one font from the **RESERVED** Jacobian $J$, which is the hazard C22 and C33 were charged for. |
| **C42** | **$h_k$**, the singular sequence of `lem:formstruct`(ii) | **Claimed, and §1.7's $h$ rule is AMENDED rather than breached.** | §1.7 reads "a subscripted $h$ is always a bandwidth". That rule is **amended, in the open**, on the C27/C20 model: the bandwidths are $h_u,h_x$, subscripted by a **variable name**, and an **integer index** is a different subscript type — the device §1.7 already uses three times ($\Psi_L$ vs $\Psi_{jk}$; $b(\cdot)$ vs $b_1,\dots,b_{L_x}$; $\sigma_g$ vs $\sigma_j(\mathcal A)$). A sequence of *directions* genuinely wants to be called $h$, and every alternative is worse: $\zeta_k$ collides with C28's subscripted-$\zeta$-is-the-triple half, $v_k$ is used in the same proof for the pre-correction orthonormal sequence, and $j,m$ are the singular index and the LF counter. **Slot swept:** no file contains an integer-subscripted $h$. |
| **C43** | **$\mathcal B_{\mathcal V}$**, the compression | **Local to one proof; every *statement* prints $P_{\mathcal V}\mathcal B_{\rm sym}P_{\mathcal V}\vert_{\mathcal V}$ in full.** | **Slot swept:** the only $\mathcal B$ subscripts in any file are `sym` and `mult`. The separation is by subscript **type** — a *word* names a part of $\mathcal B$, a *space* names the compression to that space — and the reading is *correct*, not merely non-colliding, since the compression is a $\mathcal B$-family object. **No fourth font of $B$**, which is what C33 charged. Writing it out in the statements follows the existing majority convention (3 pre-existing sites, 0 abbreviations); abbreviating inside one proof keeps six displays legible. Recorded in §1.6 and §1.7. |
| **C44** | **$g_\vartheta$** as an abbreviation for the multiplier $\vartheta'\sigma_q/\pi$ | **DECLINED after drafting; the multiplier is written out at all $\sim$15 Part G sites.** | Two reasons, and each alone decides it. **(a) Slot swept and occupied by type**: $g$ is the generic *moment weight* (C14) and it *fills the subscript slot* of $\nu_g$, $\sigma_g$, $\hat\nu_g$ — so a reader meeting $g_\vartheta$ would be invited to form $\nu_{g_\vartheta}$, which is meaningless here, and $g$ is a function of the observables while the multiplier is a function on $\mathcal X$. **(b)** An abbreviation would **re-collapse the distinction §8.4 item 2 exists to protect**: $\sigma_q$ is the multiplier, $\mathcal B_{\rm mult}$ is multiplication *by* $\vartheta'\sigma_q/\pi$, and naming the latter's symbol $g_\vartheta$ puts a one-letter name on the object C7 retired $M$ for naming. **Every existing site writes it out; zero abbreviate.** — **DISPATCH-6 AMENDMENT, two parts.** *(1)* Reason **(b) carries this row alone**; reason (a) is weaker than it was written, since $g_\vartheta$ is a function on $\mathcal X$ while $g$ is a moment weight on the observables and the types already separate them. The row stands on (b). *(2)* The decline now has a **guard**: a third declined-slot row in `check_rendering_sync.py`. See §1.5d for the guard's known limit — CHECK 3 strips comments, so the one surviving occurrence, in a header comment at `proofs.tex:2156`, was out of its reach and was fixed by hand. |

### 1.5d Dispatch-6 fix pass: four slots claimed, and one declined slot given its guard

The fix pass against `proofs_review_dispatch6.md` repaired L-21's measurability chain. The repair is
structural rather than cosmetic — the reviewer's MAJOR 2.1 was that **one symbol was carrying two
functions**, so the fix necessarily introduces the second name — and §1.5b's rule is applied to each new
letter before it is written.

| # | Slot | Disposition | Rationale, and the slot sweep |
|---|---|---|---|
| **C45** | **$\ell^\Pi_0$**, the representative A2c supplies, as distinct from the version `lem:reprsmooth` Step 2 constructs | **Claimed, and the claim is the fix.** Local to `lem:reprsmooth`'s Steps 0–2. | Step 2 previously *redefined* $\ell^\Pi_\star$ and then justified the mollification "jointly measurable by Step 0" — which had established that for the **pre-redefinition** representative. Read literally it cited a property of a different function, and of the object whose measurability was being proved. Two names make the load-bearing sentence sayable: *at each $t\in\mathcal T_\ast$ the two agree Lebesgue-a.e., so the interval average is identical at every $(u,t)$*. **Slot swept:** no `\ell^\Pi_0`, `\ell_0` or integer-subscripted $\ell$ occurs in any `.tex`; the only `\ell_0` anywhere is a coefficient in a toy illustration at `theory_memo.md:1196`, separated by the $\Pi$ superscript and living in no artifact. Subscript **$0$ = the representative fixed at Step 0**, $\star$ = the selected solution, and the two name **the same element of $L^2(\Pi)$** — which the proof says in the sentence that introduces it. Recorded in §1.6 and §1.7. |

would remain, out of CHECK 3's reach.** That occurrence is fixed by hand and the limitation is written
into the script beside the row, because the alternative is rediscovering it. The §1.5b sweep is a grep
over *all* text, comments included; CHECK 3 is the guard on the live slot. The two are complementary and
neither substitutes for the other.

All four stand, all four die in the clause or proof that declares them, and §1.7 records the subscript-type pair |

**Two methodological findings, and the second is a limit on this file's own governing rule.**

**(i) §1.5b's rule is necessary and not sufficient.** The rule is *sweep for the letter you are claiming,
not only for the letter you are renaming.* C67 **did** sweep for $\omega$ — and reported the sweep
**wrongly**. So the rule needs a second clause, now binding: **a sweep result asserted in this file must
be reproducible by a stated command, and a claim of the form "$X$ occurs in no file" must name the pattern
swept.** C67's claim would have failed a `\omega` grep at fifty-five hits; C71 states the pattern and the
referents. This is the fourth consecutive round in which the slot-register machinery has paid for itself
and the first in which it has *mis*paid.

> **DISPATCH-18 ADDITION: a third clause, and it is what makes the whole class mechanically checkable.**
> **Every slot sweep must state *as of which dispatch* it was run** — "swept as of dispatch $k$" — and a
> sweep stated without a dispatch is to be read as unverified, not as current. The diagnosis is exact and
> was found by the warden: this file and the artifact both write *"zero at the time of the claim"* and
> *"zero now"* **in identical words**, so a reader cannot tell a sweep that has gone stale from a sweep
> that was false when written, and neither can a re-sweep. The two are different defects with different
> remedies — a stale sweep is re-run, a false sweep is a finding — and the same undated sentence covered
> both. **The three cases the convention separates, all live in this project:** `\mathsf p`'s sweep was
> **true when written and is stale now** (the proof that claims it introduced the only occurrences), and
> is a non-defect once dated; `\mathsf Z`'s sweep was **false when written**, the Part-H sites predating
> it by a dispatch, and is FBC-3 — the one false claim that reached compiled text; C74's `\mathbb C` and
> `\mathbb N` sweeps were **false when written for a mechanical reason**, the corpus running two spellings
> of one symbol so that the pattern saw half of it. **A second, subordinate clause follows from the
> third:** a sweep of a *symbol* must sweep every spelling of that symbol, and where a preamble macro
> exists the corpus is unified onto it (dispatch 18; C74), so that "every spelling" is one pattern.

**(ii) A symbol can be binding *in fact* without ever being declared binding, and §1.6 is where that
hides.** $\alpha$ was never claimed as a slot by any of C1–C68; it entered through A4(ii) as ordinary
rate notation and accreted forty sites while sitting in §1.6's local list. **The audit that catches this
is not a collision sweep but a *census of §1.6 against actual usage*,** and it should be run once per
dispatch rather than at the end: every §1.6 entry whose site count exceeds a handful, or which appears in
a *statement* rather than only inside proofs, is a candidate for the same defect. $s_0$ was the other
candidate in this round and it survives the test only because C3(ii) as restated now *defines* it, which
makes it binding by declaration rather than by accretion.

**(iii) DISPATCH-22 ADDITION, AND IT IS THE CLAUSE THAT STOPS THE RECURRENCE. Findings (i) and (ii)
discipline sweeps that get *written*; nothing until now required a sweep to *exist*.** §1.5g(i)'s three
clauses say how a sweep must be stated — reproducible by a named pattern, dated to a dispatch, run over
every spelling. Each of them binds only once an author has decided to record a sweep. **The defect they do
not reach is the author who introduces a symbol and records nothing**, and that is not hypothetical: every
collision repaired in §1.5i below was claimed against a census *this file had already recorded*.
$\mathcal J$ was listed as taken, with `prp:compact` named as its owner, in §1.5h's calligraphic census,
and C77 cites $\mathcal J$ **by name** as a reason another symbol had to be renamed — and a later dispatch
used it anyway, in two statements. The four variant Greek capitals entered printed text with no row at
all. Six further letters — bare $c$, $\tau$, $\sigma$, $d$, $\mathsf c$, $\varsigma$ — entered *as bare
letters in prose*, which is invisible to a `\newcommand` check and to any sweep of a macro name, and a fix
report of an intervening round asserted on that basis that "no new symbol was introduced". **The assertion
was false and the method that produced it was the defect.**

**The clause, and it is binding from here on.**

> **A symbol may not be introduced into printed text — a statement, a definition, an assumption, a proof,
> a remark or a display — without a dated slot sweep at the point of introduction.** The sweep is recorded
> in this register at the row that claims the symbol; it names the pattern, it names the dispatch, and it
> covers every spelling of the letter, every font of the letter, and every accent and variant of the
> letter. *A symbol introduced without one is to be read as undeclared, whatever the surrounding prose
> asserts, and the burden lies on the dispatch that introduced it and not on the audit that finds it.*

**Three consequences follow, and none of them is given by clauses (i) or (ii).**
**(a) The unit swept is the *glyph family*, not the command.** A sweep of `\Delta` does not cover
`\varDelta`; a sweep of `\mathcal M` does not cover `\mathbf M`; a sweep of `\mathsf c` does not cover
`c(z)`. C80 exists because five consecutive rounds swept commands and not once swept a font family, and
the warden's own diagnosis names it: *"I swept for the symbols named in my findings and never ran a
font-and-variant census."*

> **DISPATCH-23 AMENDMENT TO CLAUSE (a), AND IT IS THE CLAUSE THAT MAKES THE CLASS FALSIFIABLE INSTEAD
> OF MERELY FORBIDDEN.** Clause (a) as written says what a sweep must *cover*. It does not say what a
> sweep must *print*, and every one of the eight false claims of dispatch 22 was a sweep stated correctly
> in form — pattern named, dispatch dated, glyph family invoked — and run over the wrong scope. Nothing
> in (a) let a later reader tell those apart from a sweep that was run correctly, because the claims and
> the counts were reported as prose. **The amendment:**
>
> > **A row's sweep must print its result as *per-file counts*: one number per live `.tex` file, for the
> > pattern named, as of the dispatch named. A row asserting "zero occurrences", "only at $X$", or "does
> > not co-occur" *without* per-file numbers is to be read as UNVERIFIED, whatever the surrounding prose
> > asserts, and a re-sweep may falsify it arithmetically rather than by reading every site.**
> >
> > Three subordinate clauses follow and none is implied by the first. **(a.1) A count is of *lines with
> > a match* or of *occurrences*, and the row must say which** — the FBC-7 distinction C88 already prints
> > both numbers for. **(a.2) A *mention* is not an *occurrence*.** Where a corpus names a retired or
> > partner symbol in the sentence that retires or separates it — the practice C80 records for
> > `varDelta`, C83 for `\mathbf M`, and this dispatch for `\Theta_1`, `\Upsilon` and `\Xi_\ast` — the row
> > prints uses and mentions separately, so that a re-sweep can tell them apart without reading the line.
> >
> > **(a.3) DISPATCH-24 ADDITION, AND IT DISCIPLINES THE *WORD* ATTACHED TO A SWEEP RATHER THAN ITS
> > ARITHMETIC.**
> >
> > > **A sweep may not be described as "full", "complete", "only", "single", "the letter's census" or
> > > any other completeness word unless the pattern printed is the *glyph family* and the count is
> > > printed *for all three live files*. A completeness word attached to a sweep that fails either test
> > > is to be read as UNVERIFIED, on the same footing as a missing count under (a), and the sweep is
> > > to be re-read as a claim about the patterns it names and about nothing else.**
> >
> > **Why (a.1) and (a.2) do not reach this, and the evidence is dispatch 23's own two failures.**
> > Clauses (a)–(a.2) govern *numbers*: how many, of what unit, use or mention. **Both of dispatch 23's
> > count failures were completeness *words* sitting on numbers that were individually well formed.**
> > *(1)* C99 printed five patterns with per-file counts, in order, dated — and called the result
> > **"the letter's full census."** Nothing in (a)–(a.2) is violated by that sentence; what is violated
> > is arithmetic no clause required, namely that the printed patterns *span the glyph*. They did not:
> > `\bM\b` cannot match `M_k` because `_` is a word character, and the Doob martingale went two rounds
> > uncounted behind the word "full". *(2)* §1.6's $\mathsf M$ row said the symbol lived in
> > `lem:srctransfer`(ii) **"only"**, and the word "only" is what excluded the four lines that are the
> > symbol's entire life. **Both are the shape (a) was written against — scope of the thing swept
> > against scope of the thing claimed — displaced from the count to the adjective.**
> >
> > **Two consequences, and the second is the enforceable one.** *(i)* A row may always say what it
> > measured; it may not say that what it measured was everything, unless the two tests above are met on
> > the face of the row. *(ii)* **The tests are mechanical**: a reader checks that the printed pattern
> > set covers every font, accent, variant and *subscript form* of the letter, and that three numbers
> > appear. **A pattern set that anchors on a word boundary fails the subscript form by construction**,
> > which is the one-character defect C90 found on `N\big(` and C100 found on `M_k`; a stem sweep is
> > therefore printed in two pieces, bare and subscripted, and every row of C100–C107 below does so.
> > *A clause that had only forbidden bad numbers would have passed both of the failures it was written
> > to catch.*
>
> **Why this and not a fourth clause of §1.5g(i).** Findings (i)–(iii) discipline sweeps that get written
> and require sweeps to exist. **They do not make a written sweep checkable by anything cheaper than
> reading every site it covers**, which is why the round-8 audit took as long as it did and why eight
> false claims survived a round whose own report asserted it had swept everything. A number can be
> re-derived in one command; a sentence cannot.
>
> **Three worked failures from dispatch 22, each of which this clause would have caught by arithmetic.**
> *(1)* C84 printed its census pattern as `N(` and claimed the Gaussian write-out was executed "at its
> single site". `N(` returns **2 lines in `results.tex` and 12 in `proofs.tex`** — and the two `thm:rootn`
> displays are spelled `N\big(`, which that pattern *does not match at all*. Per-file counts of the glyph
> family would have shown `results.tex` holding four live italic-$N$ lines against a row claiming one.
> *(2)* C80's condition (c) was checked pairwise "as of dispatch 22" and named `prp:tccfails` as
> $\varTheta_k$'s site; `varTheta` returns **5 lines in `proofs.tex`, 0 in `results.tex`, 0 in
> `assumptions.tex`**, and every one of the five is inside `prp:hatAprop`'s proof. A printed count with a
> *file* attached would not by itself have caught the wrong-proof error, but the **use/mention split of
> (a.2) would**: the row counted `\Theta_1` as absent while the proof that binds `\varTheta_k` uses it
> twice. *(3)* C83 and §1.6 both located sans-serif `\mathsf M` in `lem:srctransfer`(ii). The pattern
> returns **A 0 / R 0 / P 8 — four *uses*, all $\mathsf M_\epsilon$ inside `prp:stopfeas`'s proof, and
> four *mentions* inside change-notes — and zero lines inside `lem:srctransfer`.** One printed triple,
> split into uses and mentions, would have refuted the row without anyone reading a proof.
>
> **The clause binds the dispatch that writes it.** Every row of §1.5j below prints per-file counts for
> every letter it claims or touches, and where a count of this dispatch's own disagrees with a row it was
> not otherwise editing, that row is corrected and the correction is named — §1.6's $\mathsf M$ row and
> its $a_n,V_n,M_n$ row, §1.7's $\tau$ row, its italic-$N$ row, its variant-capital row and its $s$ row,
> and §8.9. **One count of this dispatch's own was drafted wrong and is corrected in place at C91 rather
> than shipped**, which is what the clause is for.

**(b) A bare letter in running prose is an introduction.** The rule's object is *printed text* and not
macro definitions, precisely so that "write $d$ for the degree" and "there is a finite $\tau$" fall inside
it. Six of the ten rows of §1.5i exist for that reason alone.
**(c) The rule binds the dispatch that writes the rule.** Every letter claimed in §1.5i carries its own
dated sweep in its own row — including the two claimed *in the course of repairing other findings*
($\mathsf N$ at C84, $c_\ast$ at C86) and the one whose first candidate the sweep refuted: $\eta$ was
drafted for `lem:pfreeze`'s frozen family and withdrawn when the pattern returned **28 live lines in two
readings, ten of them in `results.tex` and two of those in *statements*** (C89). *A fix round that
exempted itself would reproduce, in the act of closing it, the defect it was convened to close.*

### 1.5h Dispatch-10, Part I (`thm:rootn`): slots C73–C79, and three renames forced by sweeps

Seven rows. **Five are new symbols the $\theta$-step needs and no earlier Part had; three of those five
replace a first draft's choice that a sweep refuted, and those three are recorded as *renames* rather than
as claims, because a claim that had to be withdrawn is the more useful record.** §1.5b's rule — sweep the
letter you are **claiming** — is applied to every row, and §1.5g finding (i) is honoured: each row names the
grep pattern.

| # | Slot | Disposition | Rationale, and the slot sweep |

| **C73** | **$\mathsf F$** = the number of cross-fitting folds; **$\mathcal I_k$**, $\mathcal I_{-k}$, $n_k$ = the $k$-th fold, its complement, its size; **$\mathsf P$** = the number of independent fold partitions in `prp:medianpart` | **Claimed.** | **Why not $K$, which is what the plan and SG-13 write.** `\bar K` / $K_n$ is the *cardinality of the growing Bierens index family* $\mathcal T_{K_n}$ (B1(vi), memo §3.2, Domínguez–Lobato), and $\mathcal K$ is RESERVED for the Hilbert–Schmidt operator (C12). A bare $K$ for a fold count next to $K_n$ for an index-set size, separated only by a subscript, is the defect C29 named ("two arguments and a subscripted space do not disambiguate a symbol"). **Slot swept:** `\mathsf F`, `\mathsf P` — **zero occurrences** in any `.tex` of this project; and $\mathcal I$ is already this project's letter for a fold ($\mathcal I_a$, $\mathcal I_m$ in `lem:balance` and `def:hatT`), so $\mathcal I_k$ is an *extension* of an existing convention, with $(\mathcal I_k,\mathcal I_{-k})=(\mathcal I_m,\mathcal I_a)$ at $\mathsf F=2$ stated in `def:crossfit`. The warden was right twice, and refusing a warden finding on a sweep this file ran wrongly is the C71 species one level up.** $\IC$ is live at `proofs.tex:3422`, inside `prp:sharpquad`'s proof — *"the real field is used twice … and over $\IC$ the …"*; the field is genuinely used there, so C49's decline of the **slot** stands and is in fact *vindicated* (a symbol that names the complex plane cannot also name an integrated kernel). $\IN$ is live in `prp:compact`'s **statement**, $\mathcal J\subseteq\IN$ (`results.tex:223`), and as the mollification index $N\in\IN$ in `lem:reprsmooth`'s proof (`proofs.tex:3101`). **Why the false sweep survived a sweep that was actually run, and this is the mechanical finding of dispatch 18:** the corpus spelled one symbol two ways — the raw `\mathbb X` and the preamble macro `\IX` — so a `\mathbb`-only grep saw one half of it and a `\I*`-only grep the other. `\IN` at `proofs.tex:3101` was invisible to the dispatch-14 pattern for exactly that reason. **Dispatch 18 unifies the corpus onto the preamble macros** (`\IR,\IQ,\IN,\IZ,\IC,\IE`, defined at `paper/preambles/preamble.tex:327–332`) — 57 substitutions, **not one rendered character changed** — so that one pattern now sees the whole corpus. **Slot swept again as of dispatch 18, patterns `\mathbb` and `\I[RQNZCE]`, three live `.tex` files:** `\mathbb` → **zero**; **One methodological note, and it is §1.5g finding (ii) turning up a second time.** Three of the seven rows

nowhere, exactly as $\alpha$ was. **The census §1.5g(ii) prescribes for §1.6 should therefore be run over
the calligraphic alphabet as well**, once, and the result written down: as of this dispatch
$\mathcal A,\mathcal B,\mathcal C,\mathcal D,\mathcal E,\mathcal F,\mathcal G,\mathcal H,\mathcal I,
\mathcal J,\mathcal K,\mathcal L,\mathcal M,\mathcal N,\mathcal P,\mathcal Q,\mathcal R,\mathcal S,
\mathcal T,\mathcal U,\mathcal V,\mathcal W,\mathcal X,\mathcal Z$ are **all** in use, and

free ones.** **The calligraphic alphabet is effectively
exhausted, and any later dispatch needing a new operator should expect to write it out.**

> **DISPATCH-14 CORRECTION TO THIS CENSUS, AND IT IS THE THIRD TIME A STATUS CLAIM IN THIS FILE HAS BEEN
> ASSERTED WITHOUT THE SWEEP IT PRESCRIBES (C71, the C2 dormancy claim, and now this).** The census was
> **false at $\mathcal Z$**. Pattern swept: `\mathcal Z` — **7 occurrences**, all `proofs.tex`, all
> $\mathcal Z_0=\{z:\varrho(z)(1-\varrho(z))=0\}$ *(the binary form of the day; the region is now
> $\{z:\Omega(z)=0\}=\{z:\max_l\varrho_l(z)=1\}$ — C108)*, the degenerate region of Part C, which is **claimed at
> C34 and carries its own §5 row of this file**. So the census contradicted a claimed slot two hundred
> lines above it. **$\mathcal O$ and $\mathcal Y$ are genuinely free**: `\mathcal O` and `\mathcal Y` both
> sweep to zero occurrences across the three live `.tex` files.
>
> **The diagnosis binds on the fix, and it is not "run the sweep again".** §1.5h's own preamble asserts
> that *each row names the grep pattern it swept* — and **C76 names none**. A section that claims a
> discipline it does not apply to all of its own rows will produce exactly this class of error, because a
> later reader takes the claim at face value instead of re-sweeping. C76 is repaired below; the general
> rule is: **a row that claims a slot must print the pattern, and a paragraph that claims a census must
> print the alphabet it enumerated and the pattern per letter, or say that it did not.** This census now
> prints the two patterns it actually ran.

### 1.5i Dispatch-22 notation fix round: slots C80–C89, one font-family census, and two rules narrowed

**This is the first fix dispatch this component has ever received.** It had been floored at 0/100 for
three consecutive sweeps while every other component was repaired, and the raw score *worsened* each
round, because the text that closed other findings carried new collisions in with it. That is the fact the
round is organised around, and §1.5g(iii) above is its remedy.

**Two things distinguish this round's findings from those of C31–C79, and both are recorded because they
change what an audit has to do.** *First*, four of the ten rows are about **fonts and variants of a letter
already declared**, not about a second letter: $\varDelta$ against $\Delta$, $\mathbf M_n$ against $M_n$,
$\mathsf Z$ against $Z$, $\mathsf N$ against $N$. No sweep of a command name can find those, and none did
for five rounds. *Second*, six of the ten are about **bare letters introduced in running prose** — "write
$d$ for the degree", "there is a finite $\tau$", "fix $c>0$" — which is why an intervening report could
assert in good faith that no new symbol had been introduced and be wrong six times over.

**Every row below names the pattern it swept and the dispatch as of which it was run**, per §1.5g(i), and
every letter *claimed* by this dispatch carries its own sweep, per §1.5g(iii)(c).

| # | Slot | Disposition | Rationale, and the slot sweep |
|---|---|---|---|
| **C80** | **The variant Greek capitals.** $\varTheta_k$ (`prp:tccfails`), $\varUpsilon_i$ (`lem:bdiff`), $\varXi_n(\cdot)$ (`prp:phirate` Step 1) — and $\varDelta$ (`lem:intker`(ii)) | **One rule for three; the fourth is RENAMED.** A §1.7 row declares the family; $\varDelta$ is **written out** as $v'-v$. | **Family census, run as of dispatch 22 — and it is the census five rounds did not run.** Pattern `varDelta\|varTheta\|varUpsilon\|varXi\|varGamma\|varLambda\|varPi\|varSigma\|varPhi\|varPsi\|varOmega` over the three live `.tex` files: the last seven return **zero**, so the family is exactly four and is now three. **The rule, and it is the C20/C59 four-font device applied to a family rather than to a pair:** a variant capital is admitted only if (a) it is **bound in place** and dies in the proof that binds it, (b) it carries a **second separator** beyond the glyph — integer subscript at $\varTheta_k$ and $\varUpsilon_i$, $n$ subscript plus a mandatory function argument at $\varXi_n$ — and (c) it **does not co-occur with its upright partner**. Co-occurrence checked pairwise as of dispatch 22: $\Theta_{s_0},\Theta_1$ live in C3 and the kernel lemmas and not in `prp:tccfails`; $\Upsilon$ lives in `cor:rangegap` and its two consumers and not in `lem:bdiff`; $\Xi,\Xi'$ live in `def:spaces` and $\Xi_\ast$ in `prp:tccfails` Step 2, neither in `prp:phirate` Step 1(N2.3). **Why $\varDelta$ is renamed and not covered — this is the judgement the round was asked for, and the row was insufficient there for two reasons and not one.** *(1)* It sat in a **lemma statement**, where §1.6's "dies inside a proof" cover is unavailable, which alone would only make it borderline. *(2)* Decisively, **$\Delta$ is not a symbol with many sites but a prefix applied to almost everything in this project** — $\Delta Z$, $\Delta U$, $\Delta Y$, $\Delta\varphi$, $\Delta_{h_x}$, and `lem:bdiff` Step 2's martingale differences $\Delta_k$ — so condition (c) fails outright: a reader meeting "for all $v,\varDelta\in\IR$" is invited to read $\varDelta$ as *a difference of something*, and the consumer at `prp:hatAprop` Step 5 applies exactly that display at $\Delta_{h_x}v_i$. A separator rule cannot fix a symbol whose *reading* is wrong. **And the replacement claims nothing**: $v,v'$ is the pair `lem:intker`(ii)'s own proof already uses, two lines earlier, for the two arguments of $\partial_v\mathsf C_{h_u}$, so the write-out *unifies* with notation already present. **Residual, stated rather than left to be found:** `varDelta` now returns **three occurrences, all mentions inside change-notes** — `results.tex` twice in `lem:intker`(ii)'s note, `proofs.tex` once in the family declaration — and **zero uses**. **One methodological note, and it is the reason this round is numbered rather than folded into §1.5h.**
Four of these ten rows are **font or variant collisions on a letter this file had already declared**, and
not one of them could have been found by the procedure §1.5b prescribes. That procedure — *sweep for the
letter you are claiming* — presumes the claimant knows they are claiming. **An author writing
`\varDelta` does not believe they are claiming $\Delta$; an author writing `\mathbf M_n` does not believe
they are claiming $M$.** §1.5g(iii)(a) is the clause that reaches them, and the census it requires is the
one artifact this file did not previously contain: a list, per letter, of every **font, accent and
variant** in which that letter appears. C80 supplies it for the Greek capitals and C83 for the bold
romans; **the same census over the calligraphic, sans-serif and accented families is owed and is not run
here**, and it is handed on rather than half-done, on the ground the dispatch-18 note states — a round
that starts batching classes together stops closing any of them.

### 1.5j Dispatch-23 notation fix round: slots C90–C99, the sans-serif census, and one class closed

**This round exists because dispatch 22's *claims* failed audit eight ways and every failure had one
shape: a sweep stated correctly in form — pattern named, dispatch dated, glyph family invoked — and run
over the wrong scope.** `s'` was swept as a *command* under a rule (§1.5g(iii)(a)) requiring the *glyph
family*. `N(` was reported without checking `results.tex`, and the pattern does not even match the form
the corpus prints there. A co-occurrence check was run against the wrong proof. A row named a site the
symbol has never occupied. **The remedy is the per-file-counts amendment to §1.5g(iii)(a) above, and every
row below obeys it.**

**Reading the counts.** `A` = `assumptions.tex`, `R` = `results.tex`, `P` = `proofs.tex`; the three live
files. Numbers are **lines with a match** unless "occurrences" is said, per (a.1). **Uses and mentions are
separated** per (a.2): a *mention* is an occurrence inside a sentence that names the symbol in order to
retire, separate or declare it. Counts marked **d22** were taken before this round's own edits and are the
state the round audited; counts marked **d23** are after them, and where this round's own declaring notes
add lines, that is a consequence of a row and not evidence for it.

**Two methodological notes.**

**(i) The class, stated so that a later round can test for it.** Every one of the eight dispatch-22
failures is an instance of: *the sweep was run, and the scope of the thing swept was not the scope of the
thing claimed.* Four sub-shapes appeared, and all four are now covered by §1.5g(iii)(a) as amended —
**command against glyph family** (`s'`; and `N(` against `N\big(`, which is the same defect at the width
of one macro); **one file against three** (`N(` in `proofs.tex` reported as the census); **one result
against the neighbouring one** (C80's (c) check run on `prp:tccfails` for a symbol living in
`prp:hatAprop`); and **a site asserted rather than measured** (`\mathsf M` in `lem:srctransfer`). *Only the
last of the four is a plain error. The first three are what a correctly-formed sweep looks like when the
unit is chosen wrong, which is why the remedy had to be arithmetic and not exhortation.*

**(ii) What this round did not do, said plainly.** The sans-serif census was run for the letters this
dispatch was handed — `V, T, A, a, b, q, s, c, M, N` — and **not for the whole sans-serif alphabet**;
§1.5i's owed census over the calligraphic, sans-serif and accented families is therefore *partly*
discharged and is still owed for the remainder. The letters known to be live and not swept here are
$\mathsf B$, $\mathsf K$, $\mathsf C_{h_u}$, $\mathsf d$, $\mathsf u$, $\mathsf w$, $\mathsf D_n$,
$\mathsf F$, $\mathsf H$, $\mathsf L_\Gamma$, $\mathsf p$, $\mathsf P$, $\mathsf r$, $\mathsf R$,
$\mathsf Z$, ~~$\mathsf N$~~. They are named here so that the next round inherits a list and not an
impression.

### 1.5k Dispatch-24 notation fix round: slots C100–C107, COL-4 executed, and the completeness-word clause — **plus C108, added later by Phase C**

> **HEADING REPOINTED 2026-08-10, AND THE OLD HEADING WAS A COMPLETENESS WORD OF EXACTLY THE KIND §1.5k EXISTS TO POLICE.** The table below carries **nine** rows, C100 through **C108**, while the heading declared "slots C100–C107". C108 is not a dispatch-24 slot: it was written by Phase C (2026-08-10), which re-scoped itself to reach this file and appended its row to the standing table rather than opening a §1.5l. **Three rows of §5 ($\mathcal Z_0$, $L^2(\Pi;\IR^q)$, $\mathsf c_8$) and four further sites — §1.5f's C34 record, §1.5j's census prose, §1.7's $\mathcal D/\mathbf D/D$ font row, and §8.6 — cite C108 by name**, seven citations plus the declaration, so the slot is genuinely declared and the defect was in the heading alone — which is why this is a repoint and not a new subsection. *A reader who trusted the heading and searched C100–C107 for the register's last slot would have concluded C108 was undeclared, and that is a claim about the artifact the artifact contradicts.*

**This round exists because dispatch 23's *counts* were well formed and its *completeness words* were
not.** Every one of the five glossary defects it left is a clause that says a sweep was full, or that a
symbol occurs "only" somewhere, or that a correction "now" stands in another record — sentences that
§1.5g(iii)(a) as it stood could not falsify, because (a) governs numbers and these are adjectives. The
remedy is **(a.3)**, added above; the rows below are the first written under it, and each prints the
pattern *set* it swept as well as the counts, because (a.3)'s test is that the printed patterns span the
glyph.

**Reading the counts.** `A` = `assumptions.tex`, `R` = `results.tex`, `P` = `proofs.tex`. Numbers are
**lines with a match** unless "occurrences" is said, per (a.1); **uses and mentions are separated** per
(a.2). Counts marked **d24** were taken **before** this round's own edits unless said otherwise.
