Parenthesised superscript = stage; integer subscript $\hat\varphi_m$ = LF step within a stage (C58). **The sign of the superscript is load-bearing, and this clause was missing through round 11:** a *nonnegative* parenthesised superscript is a **stage** of `def:lfstep` ($\hat\varphi^{(0)}$ the preliminary, $\hat\varphi^{(\bar k)}$ the last), a *negated* one is a **fold complement** of `def:crossfit` ($\hat\varphi^{(-k)}$, $\hat\ell^{(-k)}$, $\hat\varrho^{(-k)}$, $\hat L^{(-k)}$, $\hat b^{(-k)}$, $\hat\zeta^{(-k)}$, $\hat\psi^{(-k)}$ computed on $\mathcal I_{-k}$). **Two different index families on one letter $k$, separated by one glyph** — which is why every Part-I sentence that names $\bar k$ says *stage* in words rather than writing "$k=\bar k$" beside a fold subscript; the two sites that did (`thm:rootn` (T-4) and Step 0 of its proof) are corrected in dispatch 12 | `def:lfstep`, `def:crossfit` |
| $\hat\varepsilon_k$, $\hat\delta^{\rm eff}_{n,k}$ | deterministic sequences | the **stagewise schedule** `eq:lfsched`: $\hat\delta^{\rm eff}_{n,k}=\mathsf c_{14}(\hat\delta_n\ln n+h_u^{-2}\hat\varepsilon_{k-1}^2)$ and $\hat\varepsilon_k=\mathsf c_{15}(\hat\delta^{\rm eff}_{n,k})^{\kappa}$, anchored at the preliminary's declared rate $\hat\varepsilon_0$. **Computable from $(\hat\delta_n,h_u,\kappa,\hat\varepsilon_0)$ before the data are seen**, which is what makes the discrepancy principle implementable; the unobservable error it majorises is $\varepsilon_k=\|\vdagger-\hat\varphi^{(k)}\|_{L^2(\pi)}$ (C65) | `eq:lfsched` |
| $\kappa$ | constant in $(0,1)$ | $2\mu/(2\mu+1)$, the Landweber exponent. Distinct from $\kappa_n$, C4′'s truncation infimum, by the subscript | `def:lfstep`, `prp:phirate` |
| $\Pi$ | finite measure on $\IR\times\mathcal T\times\{1,\dots,\mathsf L-1\}$ | reference measure making $L^2(\Pi)$ the range space; **$\Pi=\Pi_u\otimes\Pi_t\otimes\Pi_{\mathsf w}$, three factors**, the base carrying $\mathfrak B(\IR)\otimes\mathfrak B(\mathcal T)\otimes2^{\{1,\dots,\mathsf L-1\}}$ — *the third factor is a finite set with its **power set***, which is what makes B1(iv.d)'s $\varpi_{\mathsf w}(l):=\Pi_{\mathsf w}(\{l\})$ well posed and what makes $\Pi$-measurability equivalent to measurability of the $\mathsf L-1$ sections. Elements of $L^2(\Pi)$ are **scalar** functions of $(u,t,l)$. **RETYPED 2026-08-10 from the two-factor $\Pi_u\otimes\Pi_t$ on $\IR\times\mathcal T$** | `def:spaces` at `results_s2_identification.tex:71–92`, B1(iv) |
| $\Pi_{\mathsf w}$ | **probability** measure on $\{1,\dots,\mathsf L-1\}$ | the **third factor of $\Pi$**: the analyst's reference weighting of the contrast index on the *range* side, priced by B1(iv) with $\Pi_u$ and $\Pi_t$. **It is a probability measure and not a general finite one, for two stated reasons.** *(1) Arithmetic:* fixing its total mass at $1$ leaves B1(iv.c)'s $c_\Pi^2=\Pi_u(\IR)\int\bar w^2d\Pi_t$ **literally unchanged**, and with it every constant that carries $c_\Pi$ — `prp:frechet`'s operator-norm bound, `prp:compact`'s HS bound, $C_{\!\mathcal A}$; a general finite measure would inflate all of them by a factor no result tracks. *(2)* It makes the $\mathsf L=2$ reduction **exact rather than up to a constant**: $\Pi_{\mathsf w}=\delta_1$ is the only probability measure on a one-point set, so $\Pi=\Pi_u\otimes\Pi_t$ there under the canonical identification of $\IR\times\mathcal T\times\{1\}$ with $\IR\times\mathcal T$. **IT IS NOT THE NORMALISATION $\Omega^{-1}$ AND MUST NOT BE CONFLATED WITH IT**: $\Pi_{\mathsf w}$ weights the contrasts *inside the $L^2(\Pi)$ norm*, an analyst's choice on the range side exactly as $\Pi_t$ is; $\Omega^{-1}$ sits *inside the operator's integrand*, pointwise in $\Delta Z$, and is determined by the data. **NEW ROW 2026-08-10** | B1(iv.d) at `assumptions_main.tex:973–975`, `:371–377`, and the reasons at `:379–393` |
| $\varpi_{\mathsf w}$ | masses on $\{1,\dots,\mathsf L-1\}$ | $\varpi_{\mathsf w}(l):=\Pi_{\mathsf w}(\{l\})$, **strictly positive at every $l$ by B1(iv.d)**. Named on the pattern B1(iv.a) already uses for $\varpi_u$, the density of $\Pi_u$ — *both are range-side objects, and $\varpi$ is the range-side glyph throughout, against domain-side $\pi$ and instrument-side $\varrho$* (C19). It appears in `eq:Wdef` (column $l$), in the adjoint pairing as the weight of the sum over $l$, and nowhere else. At $\mathsf L=2$, $\varpi_{\mathsf w}(1)=1$. **NEW ROW 2026-08-10** | B1(iv.d), `results_supp.tex:758–766`, `:768–791` |
| $\varpi_u$ | density on $\IR$ | Lebesgue density of $\Pi_u$: bounded, strictly positive everywhere, $\Pi_u(du)=\varpi_u(u)\,du$. **RESERVED, and the subscript $u$ is never omitted** (C19) — $\varpi_u$ is a range-side object, distinct from the domain-side $\pi$ and the instrument-side $\varrho$ | B1(iv.a) |
| $\Upsilon$ | $L^2(P_{\Delta U,\Delta Z};\IR^{\mathsf L-1})\to\mathcal V$ | the **tilted difference map** $\Upsilon\Xi:=\nu_{\widetilde W'\Xi}/\pi$, where $\widetilde W'\Xi=\sum_l\widetilde W_l\Xi_l$ is the **Euclidean inner product** of `eq:adjclosed`; bounded with $\|\Upsilon\|\le2\,c_{\widetilde W}\,\bar c_\pi^{1/2}$, and $\mathcal A^\ast_{\mathrm{cond}}=\Upsilon\circ\mathcal W$. *$\Upsilon$'s domain is `lem:adjoint`(ii)'s codomain, which is what makes the factorisation a composition of maps that meet.* **THREE CORRECTIONS 2026-08-10, and the middle one is the one that matters.** The domain printed $L^2(P_{\Delta U,\Delta Z})$, the scalar space. The **transpose was lost**: the row printed $\nu_{\widetilde W\Xi}/\pi$, which at $\mathsf L\ge3$ is a product of two $(\mathsf L-1)$-vectors and is **ill-typed** — the object is the inner product $\widetilde W'\Xi$, and $\nu_\cdot$ takes a scalar (or, coordinatewise, a $\IR^p$-valued) argument. And the constant printed $2\bar c_\pi^{1/2}$, which is the $\mathsf L=2$ value: $c_{\widetilde W}=1$ there and $\sqrt2$ at every $\mathsf L\ge3$. **No sharpness is claimed for the bound**: it is a pointwise Cauchy–Schwarz bound, and $\|\widetilde W\|$ approaches $\sqrt2$ only where one cell probability approaches $1$, an event whose probability the bound does not use. **Renamed from $\mathcal B$ in round 7** — see §1.4 C20 | `cor:rangegap` at `results_supp.tex:933–974` |
| $\Xi$ | element of a range-type space | **(1)** generic argument of $\Upsilon$, now in $L^2(P_{\Delta U,\Delta Z};\IR^{\mathsf L-1})$, contracted as $\widetilde W'\Xi$; **(2)** $\Xi=\nu_g/\pi$ in `prp:nophifree` Step 0; **(3)** the generic integrable **$\IR^{p\times(\mathsf L-1)}$-valued** function of `lem:nusigma`(v), where $g=\Xi(\Delta U,\Delta Z)\widetilde W$ — *the direction to the **left** of the contrast, which is the only order that multiplies*; **(4)** the generic Borel $\Xi:\IR\times\IR^p\to\IR^{p\times(\mathsf L-1)}$ of `def:dirclass`, normed by `eq:shiftnorm` at the **Frobenius** norm; **(5)** $\Xi_\ast\in L^2(\Pi)$ in `prp:tccfails` Step 2; **(6)**, new in round 12, the generic element of $L^2(\Pi)$ at `eq:hatAadj`, `prp:hatAprop`(iii) and `def:spaces`'s inner-product display, replacing a use of the now-binding $\alpha$ (C72). One species — a generic element of a range-type space — at **the SIX sites just listed**. **THE COUNT IS CORRECTED FROM "five" 2026-08-10: the row enumerated six members and then printed the number five, in one sentence, so the arithmetic falsified itself against the row's own text.** The value types are now printed rather than left to "range-type", because members (3) and (4) are matrix-valued and members (1), (5) and (6) are not. **TWO THINGS THIS ROW DOES NOT CLAIM, AND THE SECOND IS AN OVERRIDE.** *(i)* **The site list is not complete.** The corpus's own $\Xi$ clause at `results_supp.tex:5561–5583` names three sites this row does not — `rem:rootnhyp`(1), `rem:momentread`(2) and `lem:pgence`, the last being *generic real random variables on the sample space*, a reading of a **different kind** from the other five and one this row has never carried. Completing the list is handed on. *(ii)* **The dispatch that ordered this pass reported that the corpus census "says five species where the true count is six". It was checked and it is wrong, so it is not written into the file.** That census counts **value types**, not sites; it enumerates exactly five, (a)–(e); and it *defends* its own number in terms — *"it is the reason the count is five and not four"* — with (d) declared to be (c)'s stated extension rather than a sixth type. **The corrected "five → six" above is an internal fix to THIS row's site count and is not a finding about the corpus.** *Recording the check rather than the instruction is the point: a census that has been verified and a census that has been repeated are not the same artifact* | `cor:rangegap`, `prp:nophifree`, `lem:nusigma`(v) at `results_supp.tex:685–691`, `def:dirclass` at `:1255–1263`, the corpus $\Xi$ clause at `:5561–5583`, `eq:hatAadj`, C72 |
| $\mathfrak B(\cdot)$ | $\sigma$-algebra | the **Borel** $\sigma$-algebra of the named space. **Fraktur, without exception**, so that it cannot be read as an operator applied to an argument | this file, C20 |
| $\varpi_t$ | density on $\mathcal T_0$ | density of the **absolutely continuous part** of $\Pi_t$ (which is not itself assumed absolutely continuous). Matches $\varpi_u$ by type — both are range-side densities. **Replaces a use of $\varrho_t$**, which collided with the RESERVED propensity $\varrho$ | `lem:adjoint` proof, Step 4 |
| $\mathcal N_g$ | $\mathcal X\to\IR$ | upper tail integral $\int_{\{x\ge v\}\cap\mathcal X}\nu_g(x)dx$; the derivative-parameterisation counterpart of $\nu_g$ | `lem:levels`(ii) |
| $\mathcal C_0$ | set of Borel $\tilde c:\IR^p\to\IR^{p\times(\mathsf L-1)}$ | the **admissible** $\tilde c$: those for which $\Delta Z+\tilde c(\Delta Z)\widetilde W$ satisfies `eq:gproviso` coordinatewise, where $\tilde c(z)\widetilde W=\sum_{l}\widetilde W_l\,\tilde c_{\cdot\,l}(z)\in\IR^p$ is the Euclidean contraction of the $\mathsf L-1$ **columns** of $\tilde c$ against the contrast. **A linear space**, because `eq:gproviso`'s three clauses are preserved under linear combination given A1b(iv) and C1(ii). **RETYPED 2026-08-10: codomain and contrast order — the row printed $\widetilde W\tilde c(\Delta Z)$** | `cor:udep`(c) at `results_s2_identification.tex:677–701` |
| $\mathcal N_0$ | **linear subspace** of $L^1(\mathcal X)$ | $\{\nu_{\tilde c(\Delta Z)\widetilde W}:\tilde c\in\mathcal C_0\}$ — **contrast on the right, retyped 2026-08-10** — the difference functionals reachable by residual-free weights. A subspace because $\mathcal C_0$ is a linear space and $g\mapsto\nu_g$ is linear — **the qualifier is not decoration**, since `cor:udep`(c) is a statement at a *given* $c$ and membership in $\mathcal N_0$ is the weaker existential consequence (MAJ-2) | `cor:udep`(c) |
| $\Lambda^{(0)},\Lambda^{(1)}$ | constants | $\max_{1\le l\le\mathsf L-1}\int_{\mathcal T}\big(\sup_u\lVert\ell^\Pi(u,t,l)\rVert\big)\bar w\,d\Pi_t$ and the same at $\partial_u\ell^\Pi$ — **sup inside the integral, which is the whole point** — the two range-space regularity budgets of a **generic admissible representer** (A2b). **THE TWO AGGREGATIONS ARE DIFFERENT AND THE ASYMMETRY IS DELIBERATE, and this row printed neither until 2026-08-10: EUCLIDEAN over the $p$ coordinates of $\theta$ (the $\lVert\cdot\rVert$ inside each supremum), MAXIMAL over the contrast index $l$.** *Euclidean over $\theta$'s coordinates because every consumer reads a Euclidean or Frobenius norm of the assembled object, so a coordinatewise maximum would leave every one of those constants short by $\sqrt p$ — an error that is $\mathsf L$-free and visible already at $\mathsf L=2$. Maximal over $l$ because `eq:Wdef` treats the contrast index diagonally, so no downstream bound ever sums across $l$.* The row's old $\lvert\cdot\rvert$ was a modulus on a $\IR^p$-valued object and its missing $\max_l$ was a missing aggregation, not a missing decoration. Unstarred is the generic object; starred is the specialisation. **Distinct from $\Lambda_1$** of B1(v), which is a *function* of $t$ dominating $\partial_u\lambda$ — superscripts in parentheses versus a plain subscript, and the two live one $u$-derivative apart | A2b, `cor:elldiff` |
| $\Lambda^{(0)}_\star,\Lambda^{(1)}_\star$ | constants | the same two budgets — **Euclidean over the $p$ coordinates of $\theta$, maximal over $l$** — **at $\ell^\Pi_\star$**, which is what A2′(i.a)–(i.b) asserts finite; (i.b) also asserts that $u\mapsto\ell^\Pi_\star(u,t,l)$ is continuously differentiable for every $l$ and $\Pi_t$-a.e. $t$. Together they aggregate $p(\mathsf L-1)$ slicewise quantities. The star means "at the selected solution", exactly as it does on $\ell^\Pi_\star$ | A2′(i) at `assumptions_main.tex:1247–1272` |
| $\mathcal D^{-1}_0$ | $L^2(\pi)\to\mathcal V$ | integration from $\min\mathcal X$ followed by $\pi$-centring; bounded and injective, with $\mathcal D$ unbounded | `lem:levels` |
| $c_\Pi$ | constant | $\big(\Pi_u(\IR)\int\bar w^2\,d\Pi_t\big)^{1/2}$; the envelope constant of the range space | B1(iv.c) |
| $\mathcal V$ | closed subspace of $L^2(\pi)$ | $\{\varphi:\int\varphi\,d\pi=0\}$; **the domain of every operator here** | `def:spaces` |
| $\mathcal A_{\mathrm{cond}}$ | $\mathcal V\to L^2(\Pi)$ | Fréchet derivative $T'_{\vdagger,\theta_\dagger}$ in $\varphi$; Hilbert–Schmidt, hence compact | plan §1, `prp:compact` |
| $\mathcal A^\ast_{\mathrm{cond}}$ | $L^2(\Pi)\to\mathcal V$ | its adjoint. **Range consists of $\nu$-objects divided by $\pi$** | plan §1, C16 |
| $\mathcal A_\varphi$ | $\mathcal V\to L^2(\Pi)$ | derivative at a general $\varphi$ (with $\theta$ fixed) | this file |
| $\mathsf k(u,t,l;x)$ | kernel | $\nu_{g_{u,t,l}}(x)/\pi(x)$ with $g_{u,t,l}:=\widetilde W_l\,w(t'\Phi(\Delta Z))\,f_{\Delta U\mid X_1,X_2,\Delta Z,W}(u)$; the kernel of $\mathcal A_{\mathrm{cond}}$, and $\mathsf k(u,t,l;\cdot)\in\mathcal V$ at every $(u,t,l)$. **RETYPED 2026-08-10: the row carried two range arguments where the corpus carries three, and the $g$ it is built from is per-cell.** At $\mathsf L=2$ the index takes the single value $1$ and $\mathsf k(u,t,1;x)=\mathsf k(u,t;x)$. **Distinct from $\hat{\mathsf k}_\varphi(u,t,l;x):=\hat\nu_{u,t,l}(x)/\pi(x)$**, the kernel of the *sample* operator $\hat{\mathcal A}_\varphi$ (`results_supp.tex:2623`) — the hat and the $\varphi$ subscript separate them, and this row is about the population kernel | `eq:kernel` at `results_s2_identification.tex:455–469`, `prp:compact` |
| $\mathcal F_{XZW}$ | $\sigma$-field | $\sigma(X_1,X_2,\Delta Z,W)$, the conditioning field of `prp:frechet` | C15 |
| $G_\varphi$ | $\IR\to[0,1]$ | conditional distribution function of $\Delta Y_\theta-\Delta\varphi$ given $\mathcal F_{XZW}$ | C14 |
| $\mathcal K$, $\tilde{\mathcal K}$ | HS operators | the compact part of $\mathcal B$ and of $\mathcal B_{\rm sym}$. **The two differ by the reference measure, and the distinction is live**: $\mathcal K$ is the integral operator with kernel $q\,f_{X_1,X_2}$ acting on $L^2(dx)$ (used in `lem:secondord`(i)); $\tilde{\mathcal K}h:=(\mathcal Kh)/\pi$ is its $L^2(\pi)$ conjugate (used at Step 4). Same object, two weightings — not a collision, but the superscript-free $\mathcal K$ must never appear without its space. **Hilbert–Schmidt on $L^2(\pi)$ is proved, not asserted**, in `lem:solset`(iii)'s proof, from $\|q\|_\infty\le\|\partial_u\ell_\star\|_\infty$, C1(ii) and $\iint f_{X_1,X_2}=1$ | C12, `lem:secondord`(i), `lem:solset`(iii) |
| $\mathcal S$ | $\ell\mapsto\sigma_{\partial_u\ell\,\widetilde W}$ | the selection functional of L-16: maps a debiasing weight to the sum functional of its $u$-derivative, **the contrast standing to the right of the $p\times(\mathsf L-1)$ derivative — the only order that multiplies, and the order $\psi$ itself carries. ORDER CORRECTED 2026-08-10.** **$\sigma_q=\mathcal S\ell_\star$**, and $\mathcal S$ is what SG-15 sweeps. **RESERVED for this map** (C31): the sieve span, written $\mathcal S_{L_x}$ through round 7, is now written out as $\operatorname{span}(b_1,\dots,b_{L_x})$ and carries no symbol | `lem:solset`(iii), memo §3.5 |
| $\mathcal B_{\mathrm{mult}}$ | operator on $L^2(\pi)$ | the **multiplication part** of $\mathcal B$: $\mathcal B_{\mathrm{mult}}=(\vartheta'\sigma_q/\pi)\cdot{}$, bounded and self-adjoint, with $\sigma_{\mathrm{ess}}(\mathcal B_{\mathrm{mult}})=\operatorname{ess\,ran}(\vartheta'\sigma_q/\pi)$ because $\pi\,dx$ is non-atomic. **Replaces a reinstated $M$** (C32), which C7 retired in the binding voice; the point of the rename is that $M$ meant the *multiplier* $\sigma_q$, and this object is multiplication *by* $\vartheta'\sigma_q/\pi$ | `eq:Bmult`, `lem:solset`(iii) |
| $\mathcal Z_0$ | subset of $\IR^p$ | the **degenerate region** $\{z:\Omega(z)=0\}=\{z:\max_{0\le l\le\mathsf L-1}\varrho_l(z)=1\}$, where $W$ is conditionally degenerate — **one cell carries all the mass.** **RETYPED AT C108; this row printed the binary $\{z:\varrho(z)(1-\varrho(z))=0\}$ and glossed it "where one $W$-cell is null", and the second half was false at $\mathsf L\ge3$ as well as stale.** At $\mathsf L=3$, $\varrho=(q,0)'$ has a null cell and $\det\Omega=0$ and yet lies **outside** $\mathcal Z_0$ — $\widetilde W\ne0$ there and the restriction still binds — so **$\mathcal Z_0$ is strictly smaller than $\{z:\det\Omega(z)=0\}$**, and degeneracy is $\max_l\varrho_l=1$ and not a null cell. At $\mathsf L=2$ the displayed form recovers $\{z:\varrho(z)(1-\varrho(z))=0\}$ verbatim, which is why the binary row read as correct for as long as it did. **Replaces italic $D$** (C34), which C27 gave to Part F's perturbation direction without sweeping the slot. Calligraphic, matching the set convention; the letter records that it lives on the $\Delta Z$ side | `eq:degenreg`, `lem:quantu` (the $(q,0)'$ witness sits just below that display), `lem:bierens`, `assumptions_supp.tex`'s A1 paragraph, C34, C108 |
| $L^{2}(\Pi;\IR^{q})$ | Hilbert space, integer $q\ge1$ | the space of `def:spaces` with **$\IR^{q}$-valued** elements, normed by $\big(\int\lVert\cdot\rVert^{2}\,d\Pi\big)^{1/2}$ at the Euclidean norm on $\IR^{q}$; $g$ belongs **iff** each of its $q$ coordinates lies in $L^{2}(\Pi)$, and $L^{2}(\Pi;\IR^{1})=L^{2}(\Pi)$. **The two spellings are one space and not two.** The operator's *range* is the scalar one, which is why the pairing is written on scalars; the *representer* $\ell^\Pi$ of A2′, A2b and A2c is this space at $q=p$, with $\mathcal A^\ast_{\mathrm{cond}}$ read coordinatewise, and at $p=1$ the two spellings are one symbol. **Row added at C108**: Phase 4c declared the space in `def:spaces` — resolving an INV-7 scalar/vector collision across two files — and it reached this glossary in no pass, `def:weight` having been its only corpus site before that | `def:spaces`, `def:weight`, A2c, C108 |
| $\mathsf c_8$ | constant, $=2^{2\mu}$ | the **source-transfer constant** of `lem:srctransfer`, in clause (i) and clause (ii.b) alike. **One symbol, one value — and it did not have one until C108**: the proof of clause (i) set $\mathsf c_8:=2^{\mu}$ while clause (ii.b) and its proof both print $2^{2\mu}$, so one constant carried two values inside one lemma, at four printed sites. The consistent reading is the larger, under which **(i) holds a fortiori** ($2^{\mu}<2^{2\mu}$ at every $\mu>0$) and **no conclusion moves**, no consumer reading $\mathsf c_8$ at its value. (i)'s Heinz step delivers the sharper $2^{\mu}$ and that is recorded in place rather than given a second name. Joins §1.6's integer-subscripted sans-serif constant family (C60, C68) | `lem:srctransfer`(i),(ii.b), C108 |
| $\Psi_{jk}$, $j,k\in\{0,1\}$ | $\IR^p$-valued functions of $\tau_1$ | the four coefficients of `eq:biaffine`: $\Psi=\Psi_{00}+\tau_2\Psi_{10}+\tau_3\Psi_{01}+\tau_2\tau_3\Psi_{11}$, the subscript being the degree in $(\tau_2,\tau_3)$. **Replaces $A,B,C,F$** (C33), all four of which were occupied and two of them inside the same proof. $\partial^2_{\tau_2\tau_3}\Psi=\Psi_{11}$ | `eq:biaffine` |
| $\hat{\mathcal A}_\varphi$, $\hat{\mathcal A}^\ast_\varphi$ | random operators | the **kernel-smoothed sample analogue** of $\mathcal A_\varphi$ and its adjoint: the indicator smoothed by $C_{h_u}$, the two $E[\cdot\mid X_j=x]f_{X_j}(x)$ factors of $\nu_{g_{u,t}}$ estimated by $C_{h_x}$. $\hat{\mathcal A}_{\rm cond}:=\hat{\mathcal A}_{\vdagger}$. **`def:hatA` fixes the reading; `def:hatAcon` performs the construction, at `eq:hatAcon`; `prp:hatAprop` characterises it** — bounded, rank $\le n_{\rm m}$, adjoint mapping into $\mathcal V$ (which needs C3(ii)'s boundary index, through `lem:intker`(iv)), and the **exact** Fréchet derivative of $\hat T_{h_x}$. **It is NOT the Fréchet derivative of $\hat T$, and that reading is now known to be UNAVAILABLE rather than merely different**: by `prp:hatAprop`(v), $\hat T$ is nowhere Fréchet differentiable, so "the derivative of $\hat T$" names no operator. Used in the *maintained* A6 and in C3 from round 2 with no defining display anywhere; A6 is now retired, and by `prp:tccfails`(i) **no bounded operator whatsoever could have satisfied it** | `def:hatA`, `def:hatAcon`, `prp:hatAprop`, C3 |
| $\hat\ell$ | $\IR\times\IR^p\to\IR^{p\times(\mathsf L-1)}$, random | the estimator of $\ell_\star$; $\sigma(\mathcal I_a)$-measurable, in $L^2(P_{\Delta U,\Delta Z};\IR^{\mathsf L-1})$ coordinatewise a.s. **RETYPED 2026-08-10.** **A free estimator, not a derivative:** nothing in `lem:balance` or A5(iv) asserts $\hat\ell=\partial_u\hat L$. The row was missing through round 7, which is part of why A5(iv) could print that identity without anyone owning it | `lem:balance`, A5(iv) |
| $\hat L$ | $\IR\times\IR^p\to\IR^{p\times(\mathsf L-1)}$, random | the estimated debiasing weight. **RETYPED 2026-08-10, and it was not on the sweep list that produced this pass — found by asking which other rows are weights.** **Its relation to $\hat\ell$ is a construction constraint, not a definition:** $\partial_u\hat L=\hat\ell$ holds iff $\hat L=L^{\,\hat b}_{\hat\ell}$ per `def:weight`(b), which is forward obligation (O3) of `rem:obligations` and is **owed by Step 6/7, undischarged**. The row was missing through round 7 | `rem:dirmember`, `rem:obligations` |
| $\mathcal B$ | operator on $L^2(\pi)$ | $\big(\vartheta'\sigma_q/\pi\big)\cdot-2\,\vartheta'\tilde{\mathcal K}$ at a fixed direction $\vartheta\in\IR^p$, $\|\vartheta\|=1$ — **the multiplication is by $\vartheta'\sigma_q/\pi$, not $\vartheta'\sigma_q$**; both terms must be conjugated when moving from the Lebesgue pairing of `lem:secondord`(i). **Not self-adjoint**, since $q\,f_{X_1,X_2}$ is not a symmetric kernel. **Bounded**, by $\|\vartheta'\sigma_q/\pi\|_\infty\le2\bar c_\pi\|\partial_u\ell_\star\|_\infty$. **The ONE operator in the project whose domain is $L^2(\pi)$ and not $\mathcal V$** (`def:spaces`'s declared exception): $\mathcal V$ is not invariant under a non-constant multiplication | `lem:solset`(iii), `eq:Bpi` |
| $\mathcal B_{\mathrm{sym}}$ | operator on $L^2(\pi)$ | $\tfrac12(\mathcal B+\mathcal B^\ast)=(\vartheta'\sigma_q/\pi)\cdot-\vartheta'(\tilde{\mathcal K}+\tilde{\mathcal K}^\ast)$ — **the symmetrisation, which is what a quadratic form sees.** The sharp constant of the *form on $\mathcal V$* is $\tfrac12\|P_{\mathcal V}\mathcal B_{\rm sym}P_{\mathcal V}\vert_{\mathcal V}\|$; $\tfrac12\|\mathcal B\|$ is an upper bound and $\|\vartheta'\sigma_q/\pi\|_\infty$ a lower one, the latter transferring from $L^2(\pi)$ to $\mathcal V$ through a **codimension-one compression** (`lem:solset`(iii)'s proof). **Not determined by $\sigma_q$ alone** (M-3) | memo §3.5, `lem:solset`(iii) |
| $p_{X,j}$ | $[0,1]$-valued, $j\in\{0,\dots,\mathsf L-1\}$ | $p_{X,j}:=P(W=j\mid X_1,X_2,\Delta Z)$, the **cell-$j$ propensity given the regressors as well**. $p_{X,j}=\varrho_j$ for every $j$ is exactly $W\upmodels(X_1,X_2)\mid\Delta Z$ — *the instrument does not shift the endogenous regressor given $\Delta Z$* — and **must not be assumed**. **RETYPED 2026-08-10 from the binary $p_X=P(W=1\mid\cdot)$; the corpus indexes by cell and quantifies over all $\mathsf L$ of them, cell $0$ included.** *More cells do not weaken the requirement: it becomes an equality of $\mathsf L-1$ functions instead of one.* **And at $\mathsf L\ge3$ conditional independence no longer buys what it bought at $\mathsf L=2$**: there $p_{X,1}=\varrho_1$ is necessary and sufficient for the conditional and unconditional operators to agree up to the scalar $\varrho_1\varrho_0$; at $\mathsf L\ge3$ it fixes only the comparison cell, and agreement needs a further coincidence among the cell densities | `results_supp.tex:478–507`, `rem:draftrec`(ii) |
| $(\sigma_j;e_j,\tilde e_j)$ | singular system of $\mathcal A_{\mathrm{cond}}$ | $e_j$ right (on $\mathcal X$), $\tilde e_j$ left (on $\IR\times\mathcal T$). **Replaces $\phi_j,\psi_j$** — both letters are taken | this file |
| $a$ | constant $>0$ | degree of ill-posedness, $\sigma_j\asymp j^{-a}$. **RESERVED**, and §1.6's sans-serif $\mathsf c$ slot exists expressly to keep generic constants off this letter. `rem:swintercept`'s $\Phi(\pm1)=\pm a$ breached that rule on the symbol the rule names, and is written out as $\pm\Phi(1)$ (C35) | plan §2.6, C35 |
| $s$ | constant $>0$ | Sobolev smoothness of $\vdagger$ | plan §2.6 |
| $\mu$ | constant $>0$ | source-condition index; $\mu=s/(2a)$ under the Hilbert-scale link | memo, C5 |
| $\mu''$ | constant $>\tfrac32$ | source index for ~~$\ell_\star$~~ **$\ell^\Pi_\star$** (A2c, `lem:reprsmooth`); genuinely independent of $\mu$. **Round-9 correction, in place: this row was the last uncorrected instance of a round-7 referent fix.** Round 5 moved A2′(i) onto the *range-space* object $\ell^\Pi_\star$; round 7 corrected the A2′ comment block and the smoothness-budget table and did not reach this row. Since L-21 must deliver (i.a)–(i.b), which are conditions on $\ell^\Pi_\star$, $\mu''$ is an index for $\ell^\Pi_\star$ and never for $\ell_\star$. **Found by sweeping the referent, not by a finding.** The threshold $\mu''>\tfrac32$ is not a free choice: it is the one-dimensional $H^{\mu''}(\IR)\hookrightarrow C^1_b(\IR)$ embedding threshold, which is what (i.b)'s derivative clause consumes | A2c, `lem:reprsmooth` |
| $H^{\mu''}(\IR)$ | Hilbert space | the $u$-Sobolev scale of order $\mu''$ on $\IR$ **with respect to Lebesgue measure $du$, not $\Pi_u$** (`eq:usobolev`). The distinction is substantive, not pedantic: $\varpi_u$ is bounded above and only *pointwise* below, so $L^2(\IR,du)\subset L^2(\Pi_u)$ and not conversely, and A2c therefore imposes tail decay in $\lvert u\rvert$ on top of smoothness. **Always written with its argument**, to separate it from H1's $H^{s}$ on $\mathcal X$ (C39) | A2c |
| $\Lambda^{(\mu'')}$, $\Lambda^{(\mu'')}_\star$ | constants | the **source budget** $\int_{\mathcal T}\lVert\ell^\Pi(\cdot,t)\rVert_{H^{\mu''}(\IR)}\bar w(t)\Pi_t(dt)$ (`eq:lambdamu`), and its value at $\ell^\Pi_\star$. Joins $\Lambda^{(0)},\Lambda^{(1)}$ in the parenthesised-superscript family, the superscript being the **order**; `eq:l21budget` reads $\Lambda^{(0)}_\star+\Lambda^{(1)}_\star\le c_{\mu''}\Lambda^{(\mu'')}_\star$, so the family relation is readable off the notation (C40) | A2c, `lem:reprsmooth` |
| $c_{\mu''}$ | constant | the embedding constant $(\tfrac{2}{\pi}\int_\IR(1+\xi^2)^{1-\mu''}d\xi)^{1/2}$ of `eq:embedconst`, finite **exactly when $\mu''>\tfrac32$** — at $\mu''=\tfrac32$ the integrand behaves like $|\xi|^{-1}$ and the integral diverges, so the threshold is binding and not a margin. **$c_2=\sqrt2$**, since $\int_\IR(1+\xi^2)^{-1}d\xi=\pi$; the transform-free route of Step 1′ gives the *weaker* constant $2$ at the same index, and the two must not be conflated. Joins $c_\Phi$, $c_\Pi$, $c_C$; the integral is written out rather than named, because the obvious name sits one font from the RESERVED Jacobian $J$ (C41) | `eq:embedconst` |
| $r$ | constant $>0$ | **Hölder smoothness of the propensity $\varrho$** (A4(ii)), and the source of $r_n=n^{-r/(2r+d_Z)}$. Never a direction; the perturbation direction in $\varrho$ is sans-serif $\mathsf r$ (C22) | memo §3.3, A4(ii) |
| $\mathsf r$ | $\IR^p\to\IR^{\mathsf L-1}$, Borel and bounded | the **perturbation direction in $\varrho$**: $\varrho\mapsto\varrho+\tau_3\mathsf r$. **RETYPED 2026-08-10 from $\IR^p\to\IR$.** *The range condition on the perturbed propensity is the **simplex** and not the box*: $\varrho+\tau_3\mathsf r$ must take values in $\{v\in\IR^{\mathsf L-1}:v_l\ge0\ \forall l,\ \mathbf 1'v\le1\}$ for all small $\tau_3$, which at $\mathsf L\ge3$ is a genuinely stronger requirement than $\mathsf L-1$ separate interval constraints — *the coordinates cannot be perturbed independently, because the base cell absorbs the sum*. At $\mathsf L=2$ the simplex is $[0,1]$ and the two conditions coincide. Sans-serif without exception | `def:dirclass`(a) at `results_supp.tex:1267–1269`, C22 |
| $D$ | $\IR\times\IR^p\to\IR^{p\times(\mathsf L-1)}$ | the **perturbation direction in $L$**: $L\mapsto L+\tau_2D$, typed to match $L$, **its norm Frobenius**, as `eq:shiftnorm` fixes for every object carrying one column per contrast. **RETYPED 2026-08-10 from $\IR^p$-valued, and the retyping is what makes `def:dirclass`'s products conform**: $D$ is $p\times(\mathsf L-1)$ and $\widetilde W$, $\mathsf r$ are $(\mathsf L-1)$-vectors, so the products that multiply are $D\widetilde W$ and $D\mathsf r$, both $\IR^p$-valued, and **the reverse orders conform only at $\mathsf L=2$**. Italic; C2's retirement of plain $D$ is lifted under the three-font rule (C27). **The italic slot is now clear** (C34): the degenerate region, which occupied it from Part C and which C27 did not sweep for, is $\mathcal Z_0$ | `def:dirclass` at `results_supp.tex:1255–1269`, `proofs.tex:1667–1670`, C27, C34 |
| $\|D\|_{0,h}$, $\|D\|_{1,h}$ | $[0,\infty]$ | the **shift-uniform seminorms** of `def:dirclass`: $\|D\|_{0,h}=\sup_{|\tau_1|\le\bar\tau}\|D(\cdot-\tau_1\Delta h,\cdot)\|_{L^2(P)}$ (sup **outside**) and $\|D\|_{1,h}=(E[\sup_{|s|\le\bar\tau}\|\partial_uD(\Delta U-s\Delta h,\Delta Z)\|^2])^{1/2}$ (sup **inside**). The asymmetry is forced and is explained in `rem:supinside`: the $(\tau_2,\tau_3)$ derivatives are exact, the $\tau_1$ derivative is a limit and needs Leibniz's dominating hypothesis. **Neither is priced by any assumption, and neither is a rate** — they are *membership* conditions on the direction class, and the object A5(iv) prices is $\|\partial_uD\|_{L^2(P)}$, which `def:dirclass` implies but does not equal. `lem:secondord`(iv) is therefore printed in $\|\partial_uD\|_{L^2(P)}$ with $\|D\|_{1,h}$ as a stated weakening (`eq:prodbound`), and `rem:dirmember` carries the forward obligation for the instantiation at $D=\hat L-L_\star$. **Both glyphs suppress $\bar\tau$, and the suppression is licensed by monotonicity, not by convention** (round 8): each is a supremum over an index set that grows with $\bar\tau$, so both are nondecreasing in $\bar\tau$, and `def:dirclass` now prints that. The clause exists to block one false inference — that `rem:dirmember`'s display, universally quantified over $\bar\tau$ with $\bar\tau^2$ on the right only, can be optimised by $\bar\tau\downarrow0$. It cannot: the left side grows too | `def:dirclass`, `eq:shiftnorm`, `eq:shiftnormone`, `rem:dirmember` |
| $\vartheta$ | unit vector in $\IR^p$ | the **coordinate-contraction direction**: $\vartheta'\sigma_q$, $\vartheta'\mathcal K$, $\vartheta'\psi$. **BINDING ON STEP 4**, replacing round 6's use of the local $\varsigma$ (C25) | `lem:solset`(iii), §8.4 |
| $\bar\tau$ | constant $>0$ | the radius of the perturbation neighbourhood in $\tau_1$, ~~fixed once in `def:dirclass`~~ **fixed at the head of each statement that uses it: `def:dirclass` fixes it, `lem:secondord` RE-fixes it, and `rem:dirmember` QUANTIFIES OVER it.** The struck phrase was false at two of those three sites, and the third is where it mattered — `rem:dirmember`'s display is universally quantified over $\bar\tau$. Both shift-uniform seminorms are **nondecreasing in $\bar\tau$**, which is what blocks the false inference that $\bar\tau\downarrow0$ yields a $\bar\tau$-free bound | `def:dirclass`, `lem:secondord`, `rem:dirmember` |
| $\tau_{\mathrm{DP}}$ | constant $\ge1$ | discrepancy-principle constant. **Word subscript never omitted** (C23) | A7 |
| $m$ | integer | LF iteration counter. **The moment map is $\Psi$, not $m$** (C21) | C3 |
| $\hat m$ | random integer | discrepancy-principle stopping index | C3 |
| $\bar m_n$ | integer | iteration cap | C3 |
| $\varphi_m$ | function | noise-free LF iterate; $\hat\varphi_m$ its sample counterpart | memo |
| $\mathcal V_n$ | set | **the deterministic SOURCE-CONDITION ball** $\{\varphi:\|\varphi-\vdagger\|_{L^2(\pi)}\le\bar\rho,\ \|\varphi-\vdagger\|_{H^s}\le\mathsf R\}$ of `eq:srcball`. **Not a ball of $L^2(\pi)$**, and not the retired A6's Landweber-path ball: an $L^2(\pi)$-ball is $\mathcal V(\varphi_\circ,\rho)$ (C63). Nothing in it varies with $n$; the subscript is inherited from the memo's L-19 and the statement that does vary is `lem:pathcont` | `def:srcball`, memo L-19 |
| $\hat\delta_n$ | rate | operator noise level, a **deterministic** sequence **declared** by A5(i): $\max\{\hat\delta_n^{(T)},\hat\delta_n^{(\varrho)}\}$ with $\hat\delta_n^{(T)}:=n^{-1/2}\sqrt{\ln n}$ and $\hat\delta_n^{(\varrho)}:=r_n$, hence carrying the **floor** $\hat\delta_n\ge n^{-1/2}\sqrt{\ln n}$ at every $n$. **The branches are `:=` and not $O_P$ since 2026-08-04**; five sites read the former $O_P$ form two-sidedly to obtain that floor, which an upper bound cannot give | memo §3.3, A5(i) |
| $\delta_n$ | rate | ~~$\delta_n=\hat\delta_n^{2\mu/(2\mu+1)}$~~ **$\delta_n:=(\hat\delta_n\ln n)^{2\mu/(2\mu+1)}$ — the object of `eq:deltalog`, which is what A5(ii) defines and what `eq:phirate` delivers.** The rate statement the symbol carries is $\lVert\hat\varphi-\vdagger\rVert_{L^2(\pi)}=O_P(\delta_n)$ **read at that object**; in Part I it is read at each fold, $\max_{k\le\mathsf F}\lVert h_k\rVert_{L^2(\pi)}=O_P(\delta_n)$ (`eq:foldrates`, first display, per `def:crossfit`(d)). **Round-12 correction, reaching this file one round late, and recorded rather than silently overwritten.** Dispatch 10 amended A5(ii) to carry the logarithm *inside the symbol*, because (P-f), `rem:h6bandwidth`(0) and `prp:stopfeas`(iv) all read the log-inflated rate while the clause printed the log-free one; the dispatch-11 sweep of the log-free form reached `rem:phirscope`(2), `prp:stopfeas`(iv) and two `assumptions.tex` comment blocks and **did not reach the definitional row in this file** — so through round 11 the glossary contradicted its own §7 A5 entry, sixty lines below, on the project's most-used rate symbol. **The two forms are not interchangeable and the difference is not cosmetic:** `rem:rootnlog` carries the disposition — A5(iii) unchanged, A5(iv) stronger by $(\ln n)^{2\mu/(2\mu+1)}$, A5(v) weaker, `eq:muhalf` unmoved — and A5(ii)'s second half $\delta_n=o_P(n^{-1/4})$, every appearance of $\delta_n$ in `eq:ratefloor`, and the margin identity `eq:rndeltamargin` are all statements about the **log-inflated** object. **The inflation is at the level of $\hat\delta_n$ and is applied once:** $\hat\delta_n\mapsto\hat\delta_n\ln n$ *inside* the power, never $\delta_n\mapsto\delta_n(\ln n)^{2\mu/(2\mu+1)}$ on top of it — the one-symbol-one-object collision `eq:deltalog` was written to end | A5(ii), `eq:deltalog`, `rem:rootnlog` |
| $\epsilon_n$ | rate | ~~$\big\|\partial_u(\hat L-L_\star)\big\|_{L^2(P)}=\|\hat\ell-\ell_\star\|_{L^2(P)}$~~ **$\|\hat\ell-\ell_\star\|_{L^2(P)}=O_P(\epsilon_n)$ — the rate of the representer estimator, and of nothing else.** The struck display is not a definition and not notation: its equality is an **assertion** requiring $\hat L=L^{\,\hat b}_{\hat\ell}$, which no result establishes. Round 5 corrected the referent from the level $\|\hat L-L_\star\|$, which no consumer uses, to the derivative (M-5): an $L^2$ rate for a function does not transfer to its derivative. **Round-8 correction, one level in:** this row and A5(iv) both printed $\|\partial_u(\hat L-L_\star)\|_{L^2(P)}=\|\hat\ell-\ell_\star\|_{L^2(P)}$ as though it were notation. **It is an assertion**, true only if $\hat L=L^{\,\hat b}_{\hat\ell}$ per `def:weight`(b) — obligation (O3) of `rem:obligations`, owed by Step 6/7 and undischarged. `lem:balance`(i)–(ii) consume $\epsilon_n$ at $\hat\ell$ directly and need nothing more; `lem:secondord`(iv) consumes it at $\|\partial_uD\|_{L^2(P)}$ with $D=\hat L-L_\star$, and may be instantiated only after (O3) is discharged | A5(iv), M-5, `rem:obligations` |
| $r_n$ | rate | $\|\hat\varrho-\varrho\|_{L^2}=O_P(r_n)$, $r_n=n^{-r/(2r+d_Z)}$, **priced by A4(iii) and by nothing else**. The norm is $L^2$: **no clause of the block prices $\|\hat\varrho-\varrho\|_\infty$**, which is why `lem:secondord`(iv)'s $\mathsf r$-bound is denominated in $\|\mathsf r\|_{L^2(P)}$ from round 8 (`eq:rprodbound`). Rounds 1–7 printed the sup-norm form and then read it at this rate, citing A5(iv) — the wrong tag, since A5(iv) prices $\epsilon_n$ | memo T2′, A4(iii) |
| $\tau=(\tau_1,\tau_2,\tau_3)$ | vector in $\IR^3$ | the **perturbation parameter** of `lem:secondord` and `prp:orthog`, one coordinate per nuisance. A7's discrepancy constant is $\tau_{\rm DP}$ (C23) | `lem:secondord` |
| $\hat\nu_g$ | $\mathcal X\to\IR^p$ | an estimator of $\nu_g$ computed on the main fold $\mathcal I_m$. **Its construction is not fixed here**: `lem:balance`(iii) treats it through the high-level condition `eq:nuhat` and nothing else, which is why the lemma states that condition rather than a formula | `lem:balance` |
| $\mathcal I_a$, $\mathcal I_m$ | index sets | the auxiliary and main folds of `lem:balance`'s sample-splitting convention, independent by A0. $\hat\ell$ is $\sigma(\mathcal I_a)$-measurable; $\hat\varphi$ and $\hat\nu_g$ are computed on $\mathcal I_m$ | `lem:balance` |
| $\eta_{\mathrm{TCC}}$ | constant $<\tfrac12$ | tangential-cone constant. **A6, which introduced it, is RETIRED** (`prp:tccfails`); the symbol survives in `def:a6b`'s property A6b, in `prp:tccrepair`, and in `eq:a6sieve`'s open question. Word subscript never omitted | C8, `def:a6b` |
| $\delta$ | scalar | radius in $\sup_{\|h\|\le\delta}$ | memo §3.5 |
| $\ell_n$ | sequence | `assapp4` truncation radius for $|u|$. **RESERVED** | draft `:294` |
| $\kappa_n$ | sequence | $\inf_{|u|\le\ell_n}f_{\Delta U}(u)$. **RESERVED** | draft `:296` |
| $\mathcal V_{CI}$ | set | $\{\varphi\in\mathcal V:\Delta Y-\Delta\varphi\upmodels W\mid\Delta Z\}$ | C1 |
| $\mathcal I$ | set | $\{(\varphi,\theta):\Delta Y-\Delta\varphi-\Delta Z'\theta\upmodels W\mid\Delta Z\}$ | this file |

### 7.1 The three citation registers — **NEW 2026-08-24, REPAIRED 2026-08-26, and PRINTED at `assumptions_main.tex`, in the `\paragraph` "How a citation of this block is read" under the notation block. This row is a record, not the rule's home.**

**No claim of exhaustiveness is made, and that is deliberate.** A tag is named in more places than three; the three below are the three that produced mis-citations and were repaired against. The printed paragraph carried "A tag may be named in **three places**" through 2026-08-25, which is false — see "the two positions left out" below.

| Register | Position | Carries |
|---|---|---|
| **carrier** | inside a result's `Suppose … hold` | every condition under the tag or clause named |
| **pointer** | inside a definition, **when it locates** | nothing — `def:spaces`'s bare `B1(iv)` and `C1` name the objects they use and impose nothing |
| *(not a pointer)* | inside a definition, when **constitutive** | **everything cited.** `A2b` *is* "admits a version satisfying `A2'(i.a)–(i.b)`"; `def:hatT` builds `eq:trunset` out of A4(i)'s constant. Discriminant: ask what survives deleting the citation — a pointer's object does, a constitutive citation's does not |
| **declaration** | **outside** any `Suppose … hold` | **nothing.** A **declarative half** *introduces* a symbol, a type, a carrier set or a construction and is **standing** — in force at every statement, named or not, because without it the statement has no objects to be about |

**The test — SPLIT FIRST, THEN TEST THE REMAINDER (2026-08-26).** Cut the fragment where it stops **introducing** and starts **restricting**. What introduces is the declaration; the remainder is a **clause** iff *there is a spelling of the block, with the declaration in force, under which every symbol used anywhere in the corpus still has a referent and the remainder is false*.

**What it replaces, and why.** The printed discriminant was the **negation test** — "statements stop parsing → declaration" — and *the corpus refutes it*. A4(i) was existential, so negating it left $\varepsilon_\varrho$ with no referent and stopped `eq:trunset`, $\widehat{\widetilde W}$, `eq:hatT` and the seven results built on them from parsing: the test ruled **overlap** a declaration carrying no hypothesis, while the corpus carries it as a condition at seven results, correctly. The test also turned on an **unstated reading convention** — ungenerous at A0 it returned "declaration", generous at A8's basis clause (which *also* introduces the $b_j$ that `def:lfstep` needs) it returned "carrier" — and the two readings stood three lines apart in print. Splitting first removes both: it is the *remainder* that is tested, and the remainder always has its symbols.

| Fragment | Verdict |
|---|---|
| A0's carrier $\mathcal X\subseteq\IR$, with $\underline x:=\inf\mathcal X$, $\bar x:=\sup\mathcal X$ | **declaration** |
| **A0's compact interval** | **clause** — *changed 2026-08-26, was "declaration"*. A fat Cantor $\mathcal X$ leaves every symbol named and the restriction false. Consequences: `lem:intker` now **carries** it (its clause (iv) reads it twice); `lem:levels` does **not** read it at all and carries A0's **compactness** only, its (i) now spelled with `lem:levels`(ii)'s own intersection form $\int_{[\underline x,x]\cap\mathcal X}$ |
| H1's $H^s(\mathcal X)$ classical / interpolation / no boundary condition | **declaration** — `eq:sieveinhs`, A5(vi) and `eq:jackson` would name different spaces |
| H1's infinite rank, $\sigma_j\asymp j^{-a}$, `eq:h1match` | **clause** |
| B1(iii)'s $\mathcal T\subseteq\IR^p$ | **declaration** — **the model for the split**, and the only fragment already written this way before 2026-08-26 |
| B1(iii)'s $\mathrm{Leb}(\mathcal T)>0$ | **clause** — and after 2026-08-24 it is the instance $\mathcal T'=\mathcal T$ of `lem:bierenscore` |
| **B1(iv)'s stem $\Pi=\Pi_u\otimes\Pi_t\otimes\Pi_{\mathsf w}$** | **declaration** — all introduction, no remainder; carried by no hypothesis line in the corpus |
| B1(iv.a)–(iv.d) | **clause** ×4 |
| **A4(i)'s $\varepsilon_\varrho\in(0,1/\mathsf L)$** | **declaration** — *new 2026-08-26*. A4(i) is re-spelled from "there is $\varepsilon_\varrho$…" to "**fix** $\varepsilon_\varrho$… ; the condition is…". Same admitted class of $P$; `def:hatT` reads this half and `eq:trunset` is non-empty on $\varepsilon_\varrho<1/\mathsf L$ alone |
| **A4(i)'s $\varrho_l(\Delta Z)\ge\varepsilon_\varrho$ a.s.** | **clause** — overlap, carried at seven results |
| A8's $b_1,\dots,b_{L_x}$, $\beta_1,\dots,\beta_{L_u}$ as the two bases | **declaration** — *new 2026-08-26*; what `def:lfstep` builds $\mathcal S_n$ from |
| **A8's basis clause** | **clause** — restrictive (A8's own witness: $s=1.7$, linear splines), correctly on `lem:unifop`'s line, 8 sites, untouched |

**The two positions the paragraph leaves out (added 2026-08-26).** *(a) At a proof step* — naming a tag inside a proof records where the condition is consumed and charges nothing by itself; a clause consumed at a step and named on no line is **under-charged**, and this is the register in which that rule is *defined*. *(b) Inside another assumption* — `A2'` opens "**A2 holds**, and …", so a result citing `A2'(i.a)` carries A2 transitively and a census over hypothesis lines does not see the edge.

**"Clause" covers a restrictive fragment whether or not it is numbered.** That is why `A8's basis clause` is right as printed and `H1's clause` at `proofs.tex` was not — that site names a declaration and now reads `H1's declaration`.

**Vocabulary reserved (2026-08-26).** **pointer** and **declaration** are reserved to this register table. `C5'(iii)` and `C5'(iv)` printed both words with *different* meanings — "a pointer and not a restriction", "a scope declaration about the estimator" — at `assumptions_supp.tex` (the Part-VI five-clause list) and at `lem:aerr`'s statement in `results_supp.tex`, giving two words four meanings. **The C5' pair was renamed** to **cross-reference** and **statement of scope**; the register pair is load-bearing at ~9 sites and did not move. Do not revert.

**Why the rule is printed and not only recorded.** Both halves of the two-cell predecessor lived in `%` comments — in `assumptions_main.tex`'s header (pointer half) and in the `ARCHAEOLOGY` comment under `lem:opnoise` in `results_supp.tex`, *"a block citation of the parent tag is a citation of every clause under it"* (carrier half) — rendered by no document, while three *printed* instance-defences disagreed in rhetoric about what a `B1(iv)` block citation meant. *(Cited by anchor text and not by line: this pass moved that comment's line number, and a line citation into a file the same pass edits is stale on arrival.)* This file's own precedent for an unprinted convention is the `\ref`-to-an-unnumbered-`assumption` rule, which accumulated **82 bad calls across four files** while it was true and unwritten.

**One symbol introduced by the register pass.**

| Symbol | Scope | Meaning and separations |
|---|---|---|
| $\mathcal T'$ | `lem:bierenscore` only | **NEW 2026-08-24.** Generic Borel index set with $\mathrm{Leb}(\mathcal T')>0$, **bound by that lemma's own quantifier and carried to no other result.** Verified 0 corpus hits before introduction. B1(iii)'s $\mathcal T$ is the instance $\mathcal T'=\mathcal T$; `lem:bierens` Step 5 instantiates at $\mathcal T_0\setminus N_{\mathcal T}$ and `lem:adjoint` Step 4 at $\mathcal T_1=\mathcal T\cap\operatorname{supp}\Pi_t$. Separates by prime from $\mathcal T$, $\mathcal T_0$ (B1(iv.b)), $\mathcal T_1$ (proof-local) and the retired $\mathcal T_n$ (B1(vi)); the prime is already this corpus's modification mark ($h'$, $\Xi'$, $\varsigma'$, $s'$) |

## 8. Residual hazards, recorded not resolved

### 8.0 $\hat V$ versus bare italic $V$ — **NEW in dispatch 10; the description of $V$ corrected in dispatch 18**
$\hat V:=\hat J^{-1}\hat\Sigma\hat J^{-1\prime}$ (`eq:Sighat`) is the estimated asymptotic variance.
~~Italic $V$ is a **reserved local** in §1.6: a bounded random variable in `lem:bdiff`'s proof and a
$\pm1$-valued variable in B1(ii)'s counterexample.~~ **FALSE ON BOTH COUNTS AND STRUCK: §1.6's table has
no $V$ row — the only $V$ entry is $a_n,V_n,M_n$, which is about the $n$-subscripted symbol and which
itself points at "`lem:bdiff`'s bounded local $V$" as though that were declared somewhere — and the
enumeration was short by two.**
**Census, pattern `[ ${(,=]V[ $)},.\\^_]` over the three live `.tex` files as of dispatch 18 — four bare
readings, and two of them are in _statements_, which is what makes the missing row a §1.5g(ii) defect and
not a bookkeeping one:** (a) (P6)/`lem:psubst`(i)'s $\mathcal F_{XZW}$-measurable random element, bound by
"Let" **in the lemma's statement** (`proofs.tex:528–547`); (b) `lem:bierenscore`'s conditioning random
vector in $\IR^{p}$, **in the lemma's statement** (`results.tex:352–357`), used in its proof
(`proofs.tex:1307–1311`) and instantiated at $V=\Delta Z$ inside `lem:density` Step 1
(`proofs.tex:1552`); (c) B1(ii)'s $\pm1$-valued counterexample variable in `rem:swintercept`
(`results.tex:457–460`); (d) `lem:bdiff` Step 1's bounded random variable in Hoeffding's lemma, whose
cumulant generating function is the §1.6 local $\mathsf H(\lambda)$ — *so §1.6 declares the function of
$V$ and not $V$.*
**The conclusion of this section is unchanged and its ground is unchanged: $\hat V$ is separated by the
hat, which none of (a)–(d) ever carries.** What changes is that the separation is now stated against a
measured census rather than against a §1.6 row that does not exist.
**The row is OWED, and it is explicitly handed to the collision/undeclared-symbol dispatch** rather than
written here, because (d) is one of `lem:bdiff`'s undeclared locals and declaring one of them piecemeal is
how that block came to be half-declared in the first place. A single §1.6 entry covering (a)–(d) — four
bound locals of one species, a generic random element, each dying in its own result — is the whole repair.
Retained because $\hat V$ for an estimated asymptotic variance is universal notation and because the
alternative, writing $\hat J^{-1}\hat\Sigma\hat J^{-1\prime}$ out at its six sites, costs readability in a
theorem statement. **Recommended (not executed) if the warden disagrees:** write it out; the change is
mechanical and touches `thm:rootn`, `def:onestep`, `lem:sandwich` and one line of each of two proofs.

**Dispatch-14 addition, and it is a *definitional* repair rather than a notational one.** $\hat V$ was
**partial**: `def:onestep` gives $\hat\theta^{(0)}$ a value off $\{\IE_n[\Delta Z\Delta Z']$
nonsingular$\}$ and $\hat\theta$ a value off $\{\hat J$ nonsingular$\}$, but gave $\hat V$ — which inverts
the *same* $\hat J$ — none, while `thm:rootn`'s second conclusion is a convergence in probability of
$\hat V$ and its studentised display divides by $\vartheta'\hat V\vartheta$. `eq:Sighat` now sets
$\hat V:=0$ off the event. *Careful about the event twice in one definition and not the third time* is the
shape of the defect, and it is worth recording as a species: a symbol introduced beside two total symbols
inherits the appearance of totality.

### 8.1 $\ell$ versus $\ell_n$
The debiasing weight is $\ell(u,z)$, with reference points $\ell^\Pi_\dagger,\ell_\star$; `assapp4`'s
truncation radius is $\ell_n$. These are distinct symbols and the plan reserves $\ell_n$, so INV-7 is
not violated — but a reader meeting $\ell_n$ after twenty pages of $\ell_\star$ will misparse it.
**Recommended (not executed):** rename `assapp4`'s radius to $\bar u_n$, which is what it is (a bound
on $|u|$), leaving $\kappa_n=\inf_{|u|\le\bar u_n}f_{\Delta U}(u)$. This would require amending plan §1's
reserved list; escalated in `theory_memo.md` §7, item N-2.

### 8.2 $\mathbf B$ versus $\mathcal B_{\mathrm{sym}}$ versus $\mathfrak B(\cdot)$
Separated by font only, and all three appear in Step 4/Step 5 material. Retained because renaming either
of the first two breaks a binding contract (plan §15 for $\mathbf B$; the memo for
$\mathcal B_{\mathrm{sym}}$), and because the Borel $\sigma$-algebra moved to Fraktur in round 7 rather
than the operators moving (C20). **The `cor:rangegap` operator, which also carried $\mathcal B$ through
round 6, is now $\Upsilon$** — that one had no contract and was the cheapest to move.

**Round-8 status: the rendering is now synced, so this hazard is no longer live in any compiled
artifact.** Through round 7 the rename existed in the source of record and not in
`paper/sections/theorems/lem-adjoint.tex`, where the *same glyph* $\mathcal B$ was the tilted difference
map in `cor:rangegap` and the Borel $\sigma$-algebra in `lem:levels`(ii) and `prp:kerchar` — a live INV-7
breach in a compiled 26-page document, honestly self-declared in that file's header as "DEFERRED, not
resolved". Round 8 performed both renames in the rendering ($\mathcal B\to\Upsilon$ throughout
`cor:rangegap`; $\mathcal B(\cdot)\to\mathfrak B(\cdot)$ at both Borel sites) rather than deferring a
third time. **A fourth font of $B$ was also removed** (C33): the `eq:biaffine` coefficients are
$\Psi_{jk}$. The residual hazard is what it always was — three fonts of one letter, all live — and it is
unchanged.

### 8.3 $d_Z$ versus $p$

$d_Z$ is defined here as the **effective** dimension of $\Delta Z$ — the number of non-degenerate
coordinates — and satisfies $d_Z\le p-1$ because $\Delta Z$ contains a constant. The memo writes
$d_Z=\dim(\Delta Z)\in\{3,\dots,5\}$ without distinguishing the two. The distinction is **favourable**
(it relaxes the propensity frontier by one dimension) and is flagged rather than exploited:
`assumptions.tex` states the frontier in terms of $d_Z$ and defines $d_Z$ as the effective dimension,
leaving its numerical value in the application to Stage 5.

### 8.4 The vector-valued reading of $L$, $\ell$ and $q$ — **new, and it is a correction; SUPERSEDED IN PART 2026-08-10, and the part that survives is said first**

> **C18'S RETYPING IS NOW ONE OF TWO, AND THIS SECTION STATES ONLY THE FIRST.** C18 made $L$, $\ell$,
> $\ell_\star$ and `cor:udep`'s $c$ carry **one row per coordinate of $\theta$**. The discrete
> generalisation makes them carry, in addition, **one column per contrast**: they are
> $\IR^{p\times(\mathsf L-1)}$-valued, their norms are **Frobenius**, and $\ell^\Pi$ carries the contrast
> index as a **third argument** $\ell^\Pi(u,t,l)$. **Everything below is true of the row index and is
> silent about the column index**, so read it as the $\theta$-half of a two-index statement, and read
> §4's rows for the types themselves. *The one place where the difference is more than bookkeeping is
> the **product order**, which the column index makes non-commutative; it is stated in item 2 below.* At
> $\mathsf L=2$ there is one column and this section is the whole statement.

C18 retypes $L$, $\ell$, $\ell_\star$ and `cor:udep`'s $c$ as $\IR^p$-valued. Three consequences are
recorded here so that Step 4 does not inherit them silently.

1. **The statements of `lem:adjoint`, `prp:nophifree` and `cor:udep` are written for a scalar
   coordinate and applied coordinatewise.** $\mathcal A^\ast_{\rm cond}$ acts on each coordinate
   separately, so A2's "each coordinate of $\nu/\pi$" is literally $p$ instances of the same range
   condition, and \eqref{eq:tilted} is $p$ instances of the same tilted-marginal condition. No proof step
   changes: every step is linear in the coordinate index.
2. **$q$ and $\sigma_q$ become $\IR^p$-valued**, so `prp:sharpquad` at Step 4 must be stated either
   coordinatewise or for a fixed direction $\vartheta'\psi$ with $\vartheta\in\IR^p$, $\|\vartheta\|=1$.
   **AND THE PRODUCT ORDER IS FIXED BY THE COLUMN INDEX, ADDED 2026-08-10.** $q$ and $\sigma_q$ are
   $\IR^p$-valued *because* a $p\times(\mathsf L-1)$ derivative is contracted against an
   $(\mathsf L-1)$-vector **on its right**: $q=E[\partial_u\ell_\star\,\widetilde W\mid X_1,X_2]$ and
   $\sigma_q=\mathcal S\ell_\star=\sigma_{\partial_u\ell_\star\widetilde W}$, and the same order runs
   through $\psi=\Delta Z\Delta U+L\widetilde W$, $J$'s second summand and `cor:udep`'s
   $c(z)\widetilde W$. **The reverse order conforms only at $\mathsf L=2$**, where $\widetilde W$ is a
   scalar — which is why every one of those displays was printed backwards in this file until this pass
   and nothing detected it. *This is the one item of §8.4 that a reader can get wrong without getting a
   type wrong: at $\mathsf L=2$ both orders are $\IR^p$-valued and correct.*
   **The direction letter is $\vartheta$ and not $\varsigma$** — round 6 wrote $\varsigma$, which is a
   reserved *local* bound variable and cannot be made binding (C25). The direction form
   is the better one, and the supremum over $\|\vartheta\|=1$ recovers the coordinatewise worst case.
   **But the constant is not $\tfrac12\|\vartheta'\sigma_q/\pi\|_\infty$.** By `lem:solset`(iii) the form
   on $L^2(\pi)$ is $\mathcal B=(\vartheta'\sigma_q/\pi)\cdot-2\vartheta'\tilde{\mathcal K}$,
   which is **not self-adjoint**; a quadratic form sees only the symmetric part, so the sharp constant
   **for the form on the unit sphere of $L^2(\pi)$ — item 3's constant, on $\mathcal V$, is a different
   quantity and the phrase is scoped at both sites** — is
   $\tfrac12\|\mathcal B_{\rm sym}\|$ with $\mathcal B_{\rm sym}=\tfrac12(\mathcal B+\mathcal B^\ast)$,
   and $\|\vartheta'\sigma_q/\pi\|_\infty$ is a **lower** bound on it via Weyl and the essential
   spectrum. **Binding on the Step-4 dispatch.** — **ROUND-9 STATUS: COMPLIED WITH, and the item is now a
   *record* rather than an instruction.** `prp:sharpquad` is stated at a fixed $\vartheta$ with
   $\|\vartheta\|=1$; the sharp constant is `eq:sharpconst`'s and the lower bound is bracketed away from it
   in `eq:bracket`; $\tfrac12\|\mathcal B\|$ appears only as the outermost *upper* bound of that display.
3. **The space is $L^2(\pi)$, and the form's constant is the compression's** — round-7 addition, and it
   is what gates Step 4. `def:spaces` requires every operator to have domain $\mathcal V$; $\mathcal B$
   is the one declared exception, because $\mathcal V$ is not invariant under multiplication by
   $\vartheta'\sigma_q/\pi$. The quadratic form is evaluated only at $h\in\mathcal V$, so its sharp
   constant is $\tfrac12\|P_{\mathcal V}\mathcal B_{\rm sym}P_{\mathcal V}\vert_{\mathcal V}\|$. The
   lower bound transfers because $\mathcal V$ has codimension one, so
   $\mathcal B_{\rm sym}-P_{\mathcal V}\mathcal B_{\rm sym}P_{\mathcal V}$ has rank $\le2$ and Weyl's
   theorem leaves $\sigma_{\rm ess}$ unchanged. **`prp:sharpquad` must inherit this, not the raw
   $L^2(\pi)$ norm.** — **ROUND-9 STATUS: INHERITED, and one thing it implies is now stated where a reader
   will meet it.** `eq:sharpconst`'s constant is the compression's. **The strategy memo's registered
   display, $\sup_{\|h\|\le\delta}\lvert Q(h)\rvert=\tfrac12\delta^2\|\mathcal B_{\rm sym}\|$, is therefore
   exact on the ball of $L^2(\pi)$ and an *upper bound* on the ball of $\mathcal V$**, which is the
   operative one since $h=\hat\varphi-\vdagger\in\mathcal V$. Recorded at `rem:sharpscope`(2); no
   downstream consequence changes, because what `thm:rootn` consumes is the lower bound.
4. **`eq:JSig`'s $J$ is $p\times p$ only under the vector reading, so the second summand's FORM is NOT
   coordinatewise.** $E[\widetilde W\ell_\star\Delta Z']$ is
   $(p\times1)(1\times p)$, which is what makes the sum with $E[\Delta Z\Delta Z']$ well formed. Under
   the round-2 scalar typing it was $1\times p$ and the sum was ill-formed. Found by the
   `jacobian-dims-numeric` spec; recorded as finding F-15. **`assumptions.tex`'s dispatch-2 change log
   listed A3 among the coordinatewise tags; that was wrong and is corrected in round 7.** The
   coordinatewise tags are A2′(i), A2′(iii) and N1; A2′(ii) is written with the Euclidean norm.
   **DISPATCH-13 AMENDMENT, and the hazard MOVES rather than closing.** By `lem:momzero`(iv) that summand
   is the zero matrix, so $J$'s *value* is symmetric, PSD and coordinatewise. The typing hazard is
   therefore **not** at $J$ any more; it is at $\hat J$ (`eq:Jhat`), whose second summand is a nonzero
   sample average at $\widehat{\widetilde W}^{(-k)}$ and is genuinely non-symmetric — which is what forces
   the transpose in $\hat V=\hat J^{-1}\hat\Sigma\hat J^{-1\prime}$ (`eq:Sighat`). **The
   `jacobian-dims-numeric` spec is unaffected**: it checks a dimension, and the dimension is unchanged.

### 8.5 $\ell^\Pi$ versus $\ell$ — the residual hazard the N-10 close creates

The dagger and star subscripts now attach to **two different symbols**: $\ell^\Pi_\dagger,\ell^\Pi_\star$
live in $L^2(\Pi)$ and $\ell_\star$ lives in $L^2(P_{\Delta U,\Delta Z})$. The superscript $\Pi$ is the
only mark separating them, and a reader who skips `lem:adjoint` will read $\ell_\star$ as the solution of
the adjoint equation rather than as its image. **Mitigation adopted:** every occurrence of a
*range-space* object carries the superscript $\Pi$ without exception, and A2′ names the relation
$\ell_\star=\mathcal W\ell^\Pi_\star$ in its own statement rather than in a comment. **Alternative
considered and rejected:** writing $\mathcal W\ell^\Pi_\star$ in full at every occurrence, which makes
A2′(i), A3 and the variance formula unreadable.

**What the rule does *not* say (round-7 clarification).** It is not a prohibition on bare $\ell_\dagger$.
`lem:solset`(i) defines $\ell_\dagger:=\mathcal W\ell^\Pi_\dagger$, the observable-space image of the
minimum-norm solution, and that object is correctly named under the very convention above — no
superscript, because it is not a range-space object. What was missing through round 6 was its glossary
row, now added in §4. A reader should parse the superscript as a *type marker* ("lives on
$\IR\times\mathcal T$"), not as an obligatory decoration on the letter $\ell$.

### 8.6 Italic $D$ — a retirement lifted, recorded rather than breached

C2 retired plain $D$ in dispatch 1, on the ground that $\mathcal D$ (the differentiation operator) and
$\mathbf D$ (the sieve design matrix) already used the letter in two fonts and a third meaning would be
one too many. Part F then needed a letter for the perturbation direction in $L$, to pair with $h$ in
$\varphi$ and $\mathsf r$ in $\varrho$, and used $D$ — **in breach of a declaration this file makes in
the binding voice.**

**The declaration is amended rather than the symbol moved, and the reasons are these.** (i) A
retirement is enforceable only while nothing needs the letter; a rule that is breached the first time it
binds is not a rule. (ii) Every free alternative is worse: $d$ collides with $d_Z$ and with the
differentials $dx,du,d\Pi$; $\vartheta$ is now the Step-4 direction; $\iota$ and $\upsilon$ are not
readable at this density. (iii) The three-font rule — calligraphic operator, bold matrix, italic
function — is exactly what §8.2 already applies to $\mathcal B_{\rm sym}$ versus $\mathbf B$, and the
risk here is lower: $\mathcal D$ appears in two remarks (`lem:levels`, `rem:draftrec`) and $\mathbf D$
nowhere yet in the `.tex`.

**Residual hazard, recorded not resolved.** $\mathcal D^{-1}_0h'$ and $D(u,z)$ are both symbols applied
to arguments, so the separation is font alone. If Step 6 or 7 brings $\mathbf D$ into the same section
as Part F's $D$, this decision should be revisited — at which point the direction, not the operator or
the matrix, is the one to move.

**Round-8 correction to the reasoning above, not to the decision.** The three reasons given for lifting
the retirement stand, and the retirement stays lifted. What was wrong was the *scope of the sweep*: the
paragraph reasons about $\mathcal D$ and $\mathbf D$ — the symbols C27 was *renaming around* — and the
"residual hazard" it records contemplates only a **future** clash with $\mathbf D$. The clash was
**present and inside the italic slot itself**: the degenerate region $D=\{z:\varrho(1-\varrho)=0\}$ *(the
binary form of the day; the region is now $\{z:\Omega(z)=0\}=\{z:\max_l\varrho_l(z)=1\}$ — C108)* had
occupied italic $D$ since Part C, at six sites across `proofs.tex` and `lem-bierens.tex`. C34 moves the
region to $\mathcal Z_0$. **The general rule this yields is now stated at the head of §1.5b and is
binding: sweep for the letter you are claiming, not only for the letter you are renaming.** Five of the
seven round-8 collisions were produced by claiming an occupied slot, and applying the rule to the round-8
fixes themselves caught one more before it was made (the tag "R1", occupied by `rem:R1`).

### 8.7 $\mu'$ — an undeclared symbol, **found and deleted in dispatch 11** (a defect, not a hazard)

`results.tex:4269` (the round-10 text of `cor:ratefloor`) listed the primitives as $(\mu,r,d_Z,s,\mu')$.
**$\mu'$ occurred at that one site in the whole corpus and had no row in this glossary.** The project has
exactly two smoothness indices in the prime family: $\mu$ (source index, $=s/(2a)$ under H1, §1.5e) and
$\mu''$ (A2c's index on the representer, $>\tfrac32$). There is no $\mu'$ and there was never meant to be one.
**Deleted rather than defined**, and `cor:ratefloor`'s primitive list is now stated by branch — $(\mu,r,d_Z)$
on the (R-e.1) branch, $(\mu,r,d_Z,s)$ on the (R-e.2) branch. **And $\mu''$ enters no exponent anywhere**: it
supplies the finite constant $c_{\mu''}$ of `eq:embedconst` through `lem:reprsmooth` and `cor:elldiff`, so
listing it among the exponents' arguments would have been a second error in the same sentence.

**The diagnosis, and it is the mirror image of §1.5b's rule.** The rule says: *sweep for the letter you are
claiming.* This symbol was never claimed — it was **used inside a summarising sentence**, the kind that lists
what a result depends on, and summarising sentences escape sweeps because they read as prose rather than as
notation. **So the rule needs a second half: sweep the symbols a summary sentence names, against the glossary,
before the summary ships.** Pattern that finds this class: `\mu'[^'\\]` — a prime not followed by a second
prime — **zero occurrences as of dispatch 11**. The same pattern applied to $s$, $a$, $r$, $\mu$, $\mu''$,
$d_Z$ returns only declared rows.

> **Dispatch-14 correction to the parenthesis in this section's second paragraph.** It reads "$\mu$
> (source index, $=s/(2a)$ under H1, §1.5e)". **That identity no longer holds for the bare $\mu$**; see
> §8.8 immediately below. The parenthesis is left in place with this pointer rather than rewritten,
> because the sentence it sits in is about $\mu'$ and the correction is about a different symbol.

### 8.8 $\mu$ — one glyph, two numbers, and the separation is **semantic**, not typographic — **NEW in dispatch 14**

**This is the hazard the counterexample-hunter's P14 named and it sits squarely in the warden's lane.**
Since the round-12 amendment to H1 and (P-a.3), the glyph $\mu$ carries two objects that the block itself
proves need not be equal.

| Reading | Object | Operator | Domain | Base point | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| $\mu_{\rm H1}$ | $s/(2a)$ | $\mathcal A_{\rm cond}$, **population** | all of $\mathcal V$ | $\theta_\dagger$ | *derived* from H1; index of **A2** |
| $\mu$ (bare) | — | sieve compression of the **realised** $\hat{\mathcal A}$, `eq:compress` | finite-rank $\mathcal S_n$ | $\theta_\circ$ | **primitive** at (P-a.3) |

**The binding reading, stated in the artifact at `rem:pasrc`(5):** bare $\mu$ is the **second**. It is what
`eq:muhalf`, `eq:frontier`, `eq:deltalog`, `eq:phirate` and $\kappa=2\mu/(2\mu+1)$ carry. H1's identity is
retained and is named $\mu_{\rm H1}$ **only where the two must be told apart**.

**Why this is not the usual font-or-subscript case, and why it was found late.** Every other row of §1.7
separates two objects a reader can tell apart *by looking*. Here the two readings are the **same glyph, the
same type (a positive real), and the same word ("the source index")**, and they differ only in *which
operator, on which domain, at which base point*. No typographic device reaches that, which is why the
resolution is a declared reading plus a subscripted name at the three sites that compute one and use the
other, and not a rename.

**The three sites, and the consequence, which cuts both ways.** `rem:rootnscope`(1), the two daggered lines
of the smoothness-budget table (with the sentence deducing $\mu>\tfrac56$ from $s>a+\tfrac12$ at
$a\le\tfrac34$), and the flagship configuration $(\mu,s,a,\alpha)=(2,3,\tfrac34,0.32)$ all compute
$\mu_{\rm H1}$ and read the answer into a condition on the primitive $\mu$. The bridge is
**Hilbert-scale operator interpolation at exponent $2\mu$**, which `rem:pasrc`(1) shows is licensed only at
$2\mu\le1$, which `lem:srctransfer`(ii.a) *refutes at every $p>1$ by an explicit $2\times2$ witness*, and
which `rem:pasrc`(4) **declines** to restore by proviso. **So, read literally, the theorem's own scope
remark excluded designs the theorem covers and claimed designs it does not.** All three sites are qualified
in place. **The conclusion of `thm:rootn` is untouched** — no proof step computes $\mu$ from $(s,a)$ — which
is exactly why this is a notation finding and not a mathematics one.

**Also note $a$ and $s$.** They are not neutral between the two readings: (T-5) declares $a$, $\mu$,
$\mathsf R_\ast$ and $\hat\delta_n$ **$\theta_\circ$-specific** at `def:crossfit`(b), while H1's sentence
fixes $s$ and $a$ at $\theta_\dagger$. A row that separates $\mu$ and leaves those two alone would be half
a repair; the `rem:pasrc`(5) statement names all three.

### 8.9 Italic $M_n$ against C7's retirement of italic $M$ — **SEEN AND LEFT in dispatch 17, with the expired ground replaced by a live one**

**C7 retires italic $M$ in the binding voice** — *"Binding on the Step-4 dispatch"* — because $M$ named the
multiplier, which is now $\sigma_q$; C32 then moved a second live $M$ to $\mathcal B_{\rm mult}$, and §1.6
declares **sans-serif $\mathsf M$** for exactly this species, *a generic finite bound*, in
`lem:srctransfer`(ii). `lem:pconv`(i)'s moving-threshold multiplier is italic $M_n$. **That is a breach of a
binding retirement, and it is now in compiled text.**

**Two things are true and both belong in the record.**

1. **It is not created by dispatch 17.** Italic $M_n$ was already compiled, at `lem:opnoise` Step~4, in the
   display that contrasts the transfer rule with the $O_P$ companion; $a_n$ is compiled at three further
   sites. Compiling the (P8) block made `lem:pconv` *consistent with* those sites rather than introducing
   anything.
2. **The ground on which dispatch 15 left it open has expired.** That decline read: *"the header is a
   comment block, stripped from the PDF by design, so no reader of the paper meets it."* A reader now meets
   it. The item is therefore **re-argued, not inherited**: the repair is a rename to sans-serif $\mathsf M_n$,
   joining the existing §1.6 $\mathsf M$ row, which claims **no letter and no register slot** — but it is a
   **proof-body edit at up to five sites** in `lem:opnoise`, `lem:prelimtheta` and `lem:sandwich`, and
   dispatch 17 was scoped to the conventions block. ~~**Left, named here and in both on-disk seen-and-left
   lists, with the repair specified so the next round can execute it in one pass.**~~ **THE SPECIFIED
   REPAIR IS WITHDRAWN IN DISPATCH 22 (C83), and the withdrawal is a correction to this item and not a
   re-decline of it.** Two facts, neither available when the repair was specified. *(a)* **The item was
   understated.** Italic $M_n$ was not the only live form: **bold $\mathbf M_n$** was live in
   `prp:phirate` Step 1(N2.3), carrying **no row**, separated from italic $M_n$ **by weight alone at the
   identical subscript** — the letter carried *five* forms and this paragraph named four. The bold form is
   now **deleted**, the operator it named having been unnecessary; the step runs on the polarisation of a
   form already defined. *(b)* **The specified repair would have made things worse.** Sans-serif
   $\mathsf M_n$ does not "join the existing §1.6 $\mathsf M$ row"; it separates from $\mathsf M$ **by a
   subscript alone**, which is the identical defect one font over, and executing it *together with* the
   bold form would have left four live forms at once. **With the bold form gone, italic $M_n$ stays**: its
   separations are case against $\mathcal M$, weight against $\mathsf M$, and the $n$ subscript against
   the retired bare $M$, ~~all of which §1.7 documents~~ — **THAT CLAUSE IS WITHDRAWN IN DISPATCH 24
   (C100): IT POINTS AT A ROW THAT DOES NOT EXIST.** §1.7 has **no $M_n$ row**. Swept d24, pattern
   `M_n` over this file → **24 lines before this round's own notes and 27 after them, of which exactly
   one falls inside §1.7's table** (line 789), and that one is C99's dispatch-23 sans-serif row, which
   governs $\mathsf M_\epsilon$ and names $M_n$ only as a *competitor* — it documents **none** of the
   three separations this sentence attributes to it. *The three added lines are this correction's own
   and are a consequence of the row, not evidence for it.*
   *The defect is C71's read one level up: a claim that another binding record documents something,
   asserted without opening that record.* What remains of the hazard is the one this section's
   heading names — a breach of a binding retirement, in compiled text — and it is left **with no repair
   specified**, which is the honest state, rather than with a repair that a sweep of the letter's own font
   family refutes. *This is the third time an item in this file was specified against an incomplete census
   of the letter it concerns (C71's $\omega$, C74's blackboard pair, and now this); §1.5g(iii)(a) is the
   clause written to stop it.*

**DISPATCH-23: COL-4 IS RE-DECIDED ON TRUE PREMISES, AND THE ANSWER IS THE SAME ONE FOR A DIFFERENT
REASON.** The withdrawal above rests entirely on the clause *"$\mathsf M$ is already `lem:srctransfer`(ii)'s
generic finite bound"*. **That clause is false.** Sweep as of dispatch 22, pattern `\mathsf M` over the

**Re-decision: the repair $M_n\to\mathsf M_n$ is WITHDRAWN AGAIN, on the ground the true census supplies,
and that ground is strictly stronger than the false one.** The imagined competitor was *a generic finite
bound in an unrelated lemma*, from which $\mathsf M_n$ would have been separated by a subscript alone. The

discussion.** A sans-serif $\mathsf M_n$ would therefore stand against $\mathsf M_\epsilon$ in one font,
one letter, and a subscript-type separation *between two objects of one kind*. That is worse than the
pairing the withdrawal was written against, not better. **Italic $M_n$ stays; this section's hazard stays
open with no repair specified.**

**And the census that settles it turns up a live breach that no round has named, recorded here and not

> **DISPATCH-24 RESOLUTION OF BOTH, AND OF §8.9 ITSELF: COL-4 IS EXECUTED AND THIS HAZARD IS CLOSED
> (C100).** The two false pointers above are not repaired by writing the missing §1.7 row, because the

**What is *not* a hazard, checked rather than assumed.** $a_n$ against the **RESERVED** degree of

that binds $a_n$ with ``Let''. **DISPATCH-18 ADDITION, and it is a procedural correction to this
paragraph and not to its conclusion: the slot $a_n$ was DECLINED BY NAME at C62, and this paragraph
re-argued the merits without citing the decline.** The argument above survives — C62 asserted the hazard
and did not check whether $a$ ever carries a subscript, and it does not — but the disposition had to be
recorded at C62 rather than won again here. **C62 is amended in the open in dispatch 18**: its decline is
narrowed to a *binding* name for the discrepancy inflation factor, which still carries none, and the
italic $a_n$ bound inside `lem:pconv` is licensed as a §1.6 local. Neither the decline nor the claim now
stands alone. $V_n$ against $\hat V$: separated by the hat, per §8.0 — **which, as of dispatch 18, states
the separation against a measured census of bare $V$ rather than against a §1.6 row that does not
exist.**
